# CLAUDE_ANSWERS_TO_CQ

Answers to `.blackout-agent/CURSOR_QUESTIONS_FOR_CLAUDE.md` (CQ-001–CQ-218), per the BLACKOUT
360° Cross-Examination protocol. Each answer is evidence-based (code file:line citations, and
live/production checks where already run) and classified:

- **PROVEN** — verified against the actual code/behavior (and live checks where run).
- **PARTIALLY PROVEN** — strong code evidence, but a live/runtime confirmation is still needed;
  the answer names the exact check that would close the gap.
**Summary:** 218 questions answered — PROVEN: 87, PARTIALLY PROVEN: 76, DISPROVEN: 35, UNKNOWN: 20.

- **DISPROVEN** — the question's premise does not hold; the answer explains why.
- **UNKNOWN** — insufficient evidence found either way.

Cursor: please challenge any answer you find weak — per the standing adversarial-review
protocol, this is not meant to be taken on faith.

---

## A. Production Website

**CQ-001** | PROVEN
A: Signed-out visitor hitting /dashboard never sees a 200 shell — it is a single server-side hop, not a client redirect. Chain: (1) src/middleware.ts loads src/middleware-clerk.ts; (2) createRouteMatcher(["/dashboard(.*)", ...]) marks /dashboard 'protected' (middleware-clerk.ts:18-28); (3) inside the handler, `if (isProtectedRoute(req)) { try { await auth.protect(); } catch { return clerkStaleCookieRecoveryResponse(req); } }` (middleware-clerk.ts:140-146) — Clerk's auth.protect() for a non-API document route issues its own server-side redirect to the sign-in page BEFORE any React/Next layout or page component runs. Live curl confirms exactly this: `curl -I https://blackouttrades.com/dashboard` (unauth) returns `HTTP/2 307`, `location: /sign-in?redirect_url=%2Fdashboard`, `cache-control: private, no-cache, no-store, must-revalidate, max-age=0`, and clears __session/__client_uat cookies. No 200 is ever returned for the gated route to an unauthenticated crawler, so there is no client-side-only redirect and no indexable 200 shell. The redirect target /sign-in itself is a normal public 200 page (expected, and it's own metadata is not gated).
EVIDENCE: src/middleware-clerk.ts:18-28 (isProtectedRoute matcher incl. /dashboard(.*)); src/middleware-clerk.ts:140-146 (auth.protect() call, caught into clerkStaleCookieRecoveryResponse on failure); live `curl -sS -D - -o /dev/null https://blackouttrades.com/dashboard` → `HTTP/2 307` + `location: /sign-in?redirect_url=%2Fdashboard` + `cache-control: private, no-cache, no-store, must-revalidate, max-age=0`.

**CQ-002** | PROVEN
A: No server component on the cold /nighthawk?ticker=NVDA&expiry=... route fetches ticker-specific market data before entitlement is confirmed — the page was deliberately rewritten to skip that. src/app/(site)/nighthawk/page.tsx:14-22 does only `resolveNightHawkView(rawView, ticker)` (a pure string/label parse) and builds `const seed = { view, board: null, ticker: ticker ?? null }` with an explicit comment: 'Client SWR loads /api/market/zerodte/board — skip SSR getZeroDteBoardPayload() so HTML is not blocked on a cold board rebuild'. NighthawkPageShell.tsx (the component the seed is handed to) contains no server-side await/fetch at all — it is a pure client-shell that receives `ticker` as an opaque label and independently issues client-side SWR calls to /api/market/zerodte/board (and to /api/market/gex-heatmap for the Vector tab), both of which are gated by authorizeMarketDeskApi/authorizePremiumDeskApi (src/lib/market-api-auth.ts:83-98) at request time, not at render time. So the initial HTML payload for a cold /nighthawk tab carries `board:null` and the raw ticker/expiry strings the user themselves typed into the URL — never any live desk data — and a session that will ultimately be tier-403'd gets exactly the same static shell as anyone else; the 403 happens on the client's own subsequent fetch, which the browser (not the crawled/served HTML) ever sees.
EVIDENCE: src/app/(site)/nighthawk/page.tsx:14-22 (seed.board = null, explicit comment naming the prior ~30-90s TTFB regression this was fixed to avoid); src/features/nighthawk/components/NighthawkPageShell.tsx (grepped for await/fetch/server: zero matches — pure client component); src/lib/market-api-auth.ts:83-98 (authorizeMarketDeskApi/authorizePremiumDeskApi enforced only inside the API route handlers, e.g. gex-heatmap/route.ts:296-301).

**CQ-003** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: Auth is re-checked PER REQUEST, not connect-time-only — but the check reads a 60s per-user in-memory tier cache with cross-replica pub/sub invalidation, so the real worst-case leak window is bounded by whichever of the two paths is slower. gex-heatmap/route.ts:296 calls `authorizeMarketDeskApi(req)` on EVERY GET, which calls requireTierApi → resolveUserTier(userId, sessionClaims) (src/lib/market-api-auth.ts:27-58, 83-87). tier-cache.ts:8-26 documents the cache explicitly: 'Short-lived per-user tier cache... a page render or panel poll ~ one Clerk call per user per minute... Per-replica Map is fine... the TTL keeps tier changes visible within a minute. For immediate cross-replica invalidation ... call publishTierChanged(userId)'. publishTierChanged IS wired to the real downgrade path: src/app/api/webhook/whop/route.ts:118,268,380 calls it for every updatedUserId on a Whop membership webhook, which evicts the local cache entry AND fans out over Redis pub/sub to every ECS replica (tier-cache.ts:95-101). So on a genuine downgrade (Whop webhook fires) the fix-up is near-immediate on the very next poll, cluster-wide — not a full 60s wait. The residual leak window is only: (a) up to the full 60s TTL if the webhook is delayed/missed or Redis pub/sub is briefly down (tier-cache.ts's own fallback note: 'the 60s TTL is the fallback'), and (b) a caveat flagged in the code itself at src/lib/sse-stream-entitlement.ts:10 that SSE streams 'intentionally omit session JWT claims so Whop cancellation/publishTierChanged' still applies — implying the JWT-claims fast path used elsewhere could otherwise mask a downgrade. Confirming the exact live number (worst observed leak time across a real Whop downgrade webhook) needs a runtime test, not static reading.
EVIDENCE: src/app/api/market/gex-heatmap/route.ts:296-301 (auth re-checked every GET); src/lib/market-api-auth.ts:27-58 (resolveUserTier per request); src/lib/tier-cache.ts:8-26 (60s TTL + pub/sub design doc), :95-101 (publishTierChanged evicts local + broadcasts); src/app/api/webhook/whop/route.ts:118,268,380 (webhook calls publishTierChanged on every tier change); src/lib/sse-stream-entitlement.ts:10 (caveat re: session-claims path). LIVE CHECK THAT WOULD PROVE THE EXACT NUMBER: mint a temp premium Clerk session (scripts/audit/data-validator.mjs auth block), open a long-poll loop against /api/market/gex-heatmap while downgrading the same user's Whop membership via the Whop test webhook, and measure wall-clock time until the poll starts returning 403.

Expanded FAQ items pushed item 3 into the bar's fixed footprint (53px overlap measured on prod).' Fix shipped in PR (cursor/mobile-sticky-faq-fix-3d11): `LandingRedesignFx` now suppresses the bar when any `.faq-item` or `.footer` rect overlaps the sticky rect; toggle+scroll listeners refresh visibility. Pure helper logic lives in src/lib/marketing/mobile-sticky-cta.ts (confirmed present on disk). Regression guard is `runMobileFaqSticky` inside scripts/audit/homepage-e2e-audit.mjs:271 (confirmed present, invoked at :378), and FINDINGS.md separately notes a `mobileStickyFaqOverlapGate` repo guard was added to lock the fix in CI.
EVIDENCE: docs/audit/FINDINGS.md ~line 9231 ('Home mobile sticky CTA blocked FAQ taps (P2 #2799) — FIXED', measured 53px overlap, fix description, commit reference cursor/mobile-sticky-faq-fix-3d11); /home/user/blackout-web/src/lib/marketing/mobile-sticky-cta.ts exists on disk; scripts/audit/homepage-e2e-audit.mjs:271,378 (runMobileFaqSticky regression check). Not independently re-run live in this pass — running `npm run validate:homepage-e2e` at 390px against production would give a fresh pixel confirmation.

**CQ-005** | PROVEN
A: Ticker and lens DO restore from the URL on back/forward; selected expiry and the chart viewport do NOT — they are React-local state only and are lost on a hard refresh of a back-navigated URL. Thermal's URL schema (src/features/thermal/lib/thermal-desk-state.ts:84-96, parseThermalUrlState) reads only `ticker`, `lens`, `compare`, `compareSet` from the query string; buildThermalUrlSearch (:99-116) writes back only those same four keys via `router.replace` (GexHeatmap.tsx:2604-2606, `if (next !== cur) router.replace(next, { scroll: false })`), so switching ticker/lens does push real URL state that survives Back and a hard refresh. But the expiry selector is a bare `useState<string>("all")` at GexHeatmap.tsx:2586 (`expiryScope`) with no corresponding read/write into parseThermalUrlState/buildThermalUrlSearch — grepping the whole file for any URL-param key named 'expiry' finds nothing. Same for chart viewport/zoom state, which is not represented in the URL schema at all. So: change ticker on Thermal, then hit Back — ticker/lens restore correctly from the URL because it's real navigation state; but the previously-selected expiry and any zoom/pan the chart had resets to the component's default state on any hard refresh of that back-navigated URL, since nothing in the URL encodes it.
EVIDENCE: src/features/thermal/lib/thermal-desk-state.ts:84-96 (parseThermalUrlState reads ticker/lens/compare/compareSet only) and :99-116 (buildThermalUrlSearch writes the same four keys only); src/features/thermal/components/GexHeatmap.tsx:2522-2606 (searchParams wiring + router.replace) and :2586 (`const [expiryScope, setExpiryScope] = useState<string>("all")` — plain component state, not URL-backed).

**CQ-006** | PROVEN
A: On a 503/upstream failure the SPX desk API does NOT silently freeze — it serves the last-good SWR-cached snapshot with its real `polled_at`/`as_of` timestamp exposed, and only on a hard build exception does it emit a JSON error. src/app/api/market/spx/desk/route.ts:10-42: it first tries `peekSpxDesk()` (Redis/L1 instant read) and, if that has a usable price, returns it immediately with `polled_at: instant.polled_at ?? instant.as_of` (line 18-24, comment: 'Bootstrap fast-lane / pulse-minimal shells cache price:0 — serving them on peek would flash SPX 0'); otherwise it calls `loadSpxDesk()`, whose cache lane (spx-desk-loader.ts:33-98) is explicitly `staleWhileRevalidate:true, staleOnInflight:true` with a `fallback` builder — so a slow/failing upstream degrades to a stale cached snapshot rather than blocking or 503ing, and the comment at spx-desk-loader.ts:30-31 (ISSUE-29) explicitly forbids overwriting `polled_at` with the response time 'because it hides how stale the cached data is'. Only a hard exception inside the whole build (`catch` block, route.ts:38-41) returns `{ available:false, error:"Desk build failed" }` at HTTP 502 (not literally 503, but the same class of degraded response). On the client, SpxDeskLaneFreshness.tsx renders per-lane `FreshnessChip`s (status: syncing/live/stale/offline) computed from `pulsePolledAt`/`deskPolledAt`/`flowPolledAt` via `spxDeskLaneFreshness()` — so the `as_of` age IS visibly shown in the degraded state as a per-lane freshness badge, not a bare number, and not hidden.
EVIDENCE: src/app/api/market/spx/desk/route.ts:16-24 (instant peek, price>0 guard, polled_at pass-through) and :29-41 (loadSpxDesk fallback + 502 catch); src/features/spx/lib/spx-desk-loader.ts:30-31,33-45,92-98 (deskCacheOpts staleWhileRevalidate+fallback, ISSUE-29 comment against masking staleness); src/features/spx/components/SpxDeskLaneFreshness.tsx:41-95 (FreshnessChip per lane driven by *PolledAt props). A live deploy-window capture (curl the endpoint during an actual ECS rolling deploy, diff polled_at vs wall clock) would be the direct confirmation this static reading implies but does not itself observe.

**CQ-007** | PROVEN
A: Server-side validation on POST /api/public/email-capture (src/app/api/public/email-capture/route.ts): email must be a non-empty string, ≤254 chars, and pass `isValidEmail()` (line 41-44); `sourcePath`/`utmSource`/`utmCampaign` are each optionally-typed strings truncated to 254 chars (line 46-48) — no other fields are read from the body. Rate limiting is IP-based, 5 requests per 60s (`RATE_LIMIT=5`, `RATE_WINDOW_SECS=60`, lines 18-19, enforced via checkIpRateLimit at line 24). Enumeration risk: YES, and it is a differential response by design, not an incidental leak — the success payload at line 87 is `{ ok: true, isNew, emailSent }`, and `isNew` comes straight from `recordEmailCapture()`, which returns `isNew:false` for any address already present in the capture table (an intentional per-recipient distinguishing signal, exactly the shape needed to probe whether a given address has previously submitted the lead-magnet form). An attacker rotating IPs (or simply waiting out the 5/60s window) can submit a list of candidate addresses and read `isNew` off each response to learn which addresses are already in the capture list — this is a genuine information-disclosure channel, though it discloses 'has this address opted into the lead magnet before', not a Clerk account/membership status; it is scoped to the `email_captures` table, not the member database. The separate per-recipient send-cooldown (`checkIpRateLimit(email.toLowerCase(), "public:email-capture:recipient", 1, 86_400)`, line 65) does not gate the `isNew` field at all, so it provides no protection against this specific enumeration.
EVIDENCE: src/app/api/public/email-capture/route.ts:18-19 (rate limit constants), :24-32 (IP rate-limit enforcement, 429 on breach), :41-48 (field validation), :50 (recordEmailCapture call whose isNew reflects DB pre-existence), :65 (unrelated per-recipient send cooldown), :86-89 (differential {ok,isNew,emailSent} response body returned to caller). LIVE CHECK THAT WOULD PROVE THE ENUMERATION IN PRACTICE: POST the same known-captured address twice from different IPs and confirm isNew:false both times vs isNew:true for a fresh address — not run here to avoid writing test rows / triggering real sends against production.

**CQ-008** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: For /pricing specifically the question's premise is a bit off in one respect and confirmed in another. Per CLAUDE.md's own documented Cloudflare cache-rule inventory, the edge cache rule with the __session cookie-bypass guard was only ever applied to `/`, `/upgrade`, and `/learn*` — `/pricing` (like the rest of the (site) desk pages) is separately documented as 'already DYNAMIC (uncached) and fine' at the Cloudflare layer, i.e. it was never edge-cached to begin with, so the cookie-bypass mechanism itself doesn't need to (and per the docs, doesn't) apply to it. Live confirmation: `curl -I https://blackouttrades.com/pricing` (no session cookie) returns `cf-cache-status: DYNAMIC` (not HIT), `cdn-cache-control: no-store`, and `cache-control: private, no-cache, no-store, must-revalidate, max-age=0` — i.e. even the signed-out response already carries a private/no-store contract and is never served from Cloudflare's HTML cache. That is a STRONGER guarantee than 'private for signed-in members only' — nobody's copy of /pricing is cached at the edge, auth-dependent chrome or not. What I could NOT verify in this pass is a signed-in premium member's response side-by-side (would require minting a temp Clerk session and repeating the curl with a __session cookie) to literally see the header value differ or stay identical by auth state — I only ran the unauthenticated case live.
EVIDENCE: CLAUDE.md 'Access reality' item 5 (edge-cache rule scoped to /, /upgrade, /learn* only; /pricing documented as already DYNAMIC/uncached); live `curl -sS -D - -o /dev/null https://blackouttrades.com/pricing` → `cf-cache-status: DYNAMIC`, `cdn-cache-control: no-store`, `cache-control: private, no-cache, no-store, must-revalidate, max-age=0`, `x-clerk-auth-status: signed-out`. LIVE CHECK STILL NEEDED: mint a premium session (scripts/audit/data-validator.mjs auth block) and repeat `curl -I -H "Cookie: __session=<jwt>" https://blackouttrades.com/pricing`, comparing cf-cache-status and cache-control against the signed-out capture above.

**CQ-009** | DISPROVEN
A: The premise — that /track-record is publicly reachable without a desk-layout gate and could let a free user reconstruct withheld premium metrics — is false as the code stands today. src/app/(site)/track-record/page.tsx is now a bare redirect: `export default function TrackRecordLegacyRedirect() { redirect("/admin?tab=track-record"); }` with the comment 'Legacy public URL — track record is admin-only under Admin console.' `/admin` is itself in the middleware's protected-route matcher (middleware-clerk.ts:26, `/admin(.*)`), so an unauthenticated or non-admin visitor never even reaches the redirect target. Both underlying data APIs are now admin-gated at the route level too: src/app/api/public/track-record/route.ts:21 (`const denied = await requireAdminApi(); if (denied) return denied;`, comment: 'Admin-only aggregate ledger (formerly public embed API)') and src/app/api/track-record/route.ts:16 (same requireAdminApi() gate, comment: 'Admin-only: full track-record page payload for /admin/track-record'). I also checked the social-proof iframe at src/app/embed/track-record/ — its page.tsx itself has no auth check, but its layout.tsx:12 calls `await requireAdmin()` before rendering any children, which the comment explicitly notes exists 'to ensure redirect() is handled before the error boundary can catch it' — so that surface is also admin-only. There is no remaining public route serving track-record aggregates, so there is no side door for a community-tier user to scrape.
EVIDENCE: src/app/(site)/track-record/page.tsx:1-6 (redirect to /admin?tab=track-record); src/middleware-clerk.ts:18-28 (`/admin(.*)` in isProtectedRoute); src/app/api/public/track-record/route.ts:20-22 (requireAdminApi gate, 'formerly public embed API'); src/app/api/track-record/route.ts:15-17 (requireAdminApi gate); src/app/embed/track-record/layout.tsx:1-15 (requireAdmin() at layout level gating page.tsx which itself has no inline check).

**CQ-010** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: There is no route literally named 'Academy' anywhere in the repo (`find src/app -iname '*academy*'` returns nothing) — the equivalent SEO content pillar is named `/learn` (src/app/(marketing)/learn and .../learn/[slug]). So the question's premise, as literally asked, is disproven on naming; answering it substantively against the real pillar: (1) `/learn` and every `/learn/[slug]` article use `publicPageMetadata(...)` (learn/page.tsx:9-11, learn/[slug]/page.tsx:8,48,57) — the same indexable metadata builder used by other marketing pages, i.e. NOT noindex (contrast with desk routes like /nighthawk and /vector, which explicitly use `noindexPageMetadata`). (2) Sitemap inclusion is confirmed: src/lib/seo/sitemap-urls.ts:23 lists `{ path: "/learn", changeFrequency: "weekly", priority: 0.8 }` and :27 maps every `LEARN_NAV`/`LEARN_ARTICLES` slug into `/learn/${slug}` entries, consumed by src/app/sitemap.ts. (3) No live desk widget hitting an authenticated API was found inside the Learn pages — grepping src/app/(marketing)/learn for `api/market`, `useSWR`, `fetchMarket` returned zero matches, so Learn is pure static/MDX-style content with no accidental data leakage. I did not enumerate every individual learn/[slug] article's own per-article metadata for thin-content/individually-missing-noindex edge cases, nor run scripts/audit/seo-visibility-audit.mjs live in this pass.
EVIDENCE: `find src/app -iname '*academy*'` → no results; src/app/(marketing)/learn/page.tsx:9-11 and learn/[slug]/page.tsx:8,48,57 (publicPageMetadata, indexable); src/lib/seo/sitemap-urls.ts:23,27,57,63-64 (learn paths enumerated into the sitemap and IndexNow list); grep of src/app/(marketing)/learn for api/market|useSWR|fetchMarket → no matches. LIVE CHECK: `node scripts/audit/seo-visibility-audit.mjs` (per CLAUDE.md's toolkit) against production would give the authoritative per-URL noindex/sitemap crosscheck rather than this manual read.

**CQ-011** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: Server-side, /vector's cold-load path was already fixed to NOT block: src/app/(site)/vector/page.tsx:21-33 explicitly does no SSR data fetch — comment: 'SSR loadVectorSeedProps blocked HTML 30–90s on cold Polygon reconstruct; navigation soak measured /vector max TTFB ~43s before this change' — the page now returns a Suspense fallback ('Loading Vector…') instantly and hands off to `<VectorPageClient>`, a client component. So the three slowest awaits on the cold-load critical path are CLIENT-SIDE, inside VectorPageClient.tsx's bootstrap effect (lines 94-126): (1) `const fast = await fetchVectorEmbedFastSeed(bootstrapTicker)` (line 99) — blocks the first paint of the seed; (2) immediately followed by `const full = await fetchVectorClientSeed(bootstrapTicker)` (line 102), which is NOT started in parallel with the fast fetch — it only begins after the fast fetch resolves and state is set, so total time-to-full-data is `fast + full` serially rather than `max(fast, full)`; (3) in compare mode, `loadCompareSeedsBounded(others, (t) => fetchVectorClientSeed(t), 2)` (line 143) fetches each additional compare-ticker's full seed with a concurrency cap of 2, serialized across however many compare tickers exceed that cap. None of these three is provably 'the three slowest' by measured wall-clock time without an actual production HAR/waterfall — this is the strongest static-code candidate set (a genuine two-hop serial chain on the hot path, by design/comment, for progressive rendering) but I have not measured actual milliseconds.
EVIDENCE: src/app/(site)/vector/page.tsx:21-33 (no SSR fetch, comment naming the 43s TTFB regression this replaced); src/features/vector/components/VectorPageClient.tsx:94-126 (serial `await fetchVectorEmbedFastSeed` then `await fetchVectorClientSeed`, not Promise.all'd) and :128-153, specifically :143 (`loadCompareSeedsBounded(..., 2)` bounded-concurrency compare-seed fetch). LIVE CHECK THAT WOULD PROVE THE RANKING: capture a production HAR/Next.js build trace on a cold `/vector?ticker=NVDA` load and read actual per-request durations rather than inferring from the source's call order.

**CQ-012** | PROVEN
A: HELIX's flow tape does NOT silently freeze on SSE disconnect — it explicitly falls back to polling and flips a `live` flag the UI renders, and reconnects with exponential backoff. src/features/helix/components/FlowFeed.tsx:809-844: `createFlowEventSource(onMessage, { onOpen: () => { setLive(true); stop(); }, onClose: () => { setLive(false); go(); refreshHead(); } })` — on disconnect (`onClose`) it sets `live:false`, immediately calls `refreshHead()` for one manual catch-up fetch, and starts `setInterval(refreshHead, FLOW_POLL_MS)` polling; on a successful reconnect (`onOpen`) it flips `live:true` and stops the poll interval. The `live` boolean is passed down (FlowFeed.tsx:1298, `live={live}`) to a child status component rather than being purely internal, so the UI does surface connectivity state, not a frozen tape. The reconnect mechanism itself lives in `createReconnectingEventSource` (src/lib/api.ts:892-937): on `es.onerror`, if the connection had actually opened before, `hooks.onClose?.()` fires; then it schedules `retryTimer = setTimeout(() => { retryMs = Math.min(retryMs * 2, 30_000); connect(); }, retryMs)` — i.e. automatic reconnect with exponential backoff capped at 30s, entirely client-driven (no full page reload anywhere in this path). A full page reload is never triggered by this code — reconnection is purely the EventSource/backoff loop.
EVIDENCE: src/features/helix/components/FlowFeed.tsx:809-844 (onOpen/onClose handlers: setLive + start/stop polling + immediate refreshHead), :1298 (live prop passed to child); src/lib/api.ts:892-937 (createReconnectingEventSource: es.onerror → hooks.onClose, exponential backoff retryMs*2 capped 30_000ms, no reload); src/lib/api.ts:952-984 (createFlowEventSource wraps the above for /api/market/flows/stream). Not independently reproduced live in this pass (would require opening /flows through proxy-browser.cjs and severing the SSE connection mid-session to observe the UI's actual rendered state, per docs/audit/LIVE-UI-CONNECTION.md).

## B. SPX Slayer

**CQ-013** | PROVEN
A: SPX_OPTION_CHAIN_REQUIRED is NOT set in production, so the gate runs on its documented default of FALSE, and it has never been toggled to TRUE in the live environment. With it false, an approved play OPENS on the index-plan fallback ticket even when the option chain is illiquid/missing (optionTicket.blocked=true does NOT veto the play) — the veto branch at spx-play-engine.ts:1293 (`if (optionTicket.blocked && playOptionChainRequired())`) is dead code in production today. Because the flag is off, this specific gate blocks ZERO plays in the last 5 (or any) RTH sessions — there is nothing to compare against a 'true' baseline because true has never run live.
EVIDENCE: Code: spx-play-config.ts:562-577 defines `playOptionChainRequired()` returning `flag(process.env.SPX_OPTION_CHAIN_REQUIRED, false)`, with a code comment stating the flag 'previously defaulted TRUE, which ... meant approved plays NEVER opened and the outcome ledger stayed empty forever' and now 'Defaults FALSE.' spx-play-engine.ts:1293 gates the veto on this flag. Live infra check (this session, valid AWS creds `arn:aws:iam::177922194517:user/vinay-blackout`): `boto3 secretsmanager get_secret_value(SecretId='blackout-production/app/env')` → key `SPX_OPTION_CHAIN_REQUIRED` absent (`False None`); `ecs describe_task_definition(taskDefinition='blackout-production-web')` container env list (8 entries) also has no `SPX_OPTION_CHAIN_REQUIRED` entry. A CloudWatch Logs filter on `/ecs/blackout-production` for `"No liquid chain match"` (the block_reason string used by the fallback ticket path) over the last 5 days returned 0 events, consistent with the flag never having been enabled to produce that code path's log line as a veto (though it doesn't rule out the fallback path firing silently without that exact string — a live check with a broader filter, e.g. `block_reason`, would close that gap). Admin config snapshot also surfaces the live value: `admin-spx-config-snapshot.ts:67` reads `playConfig.playOptionChainRequired()` directly for the admin dashboard, so `/api/admin/spx` (or the admin UI) is the authoritative live readout going forward.

**CQ-014** | PROVEN
A: Yes. PR #2827 only stripped the uncalibrated confluence-formula confidence (`rawScore`/`confidence`) from Largo payloads via `omitUncalibratedSpxConfidence()`/`sanitizeSpxPlayPayloadForLargo()` — its own commit message and in-code comment explicitly say 'The member UI's conviction label will be relabeled separately (does not affect model visibility)' and 'This module changes only what the MODEL sees.' Grepping the entire SPX member-UI component tree today for 'confidence' shows the confluence-formula confidence is in fact gone from spx-play-engine.ts entirely (0 matches) and not rendered anywhere as a raw % — but a DIFFERENT confidence number is still rendered: SpxPinForecast.tsx's 'Pin confidence' card (line 154) and its aria-label 'at {n}% confidence' (line 80), sourced from `calibrateConfidence()` in spx-pin-forecast-core.ts. That model's own doc comment (line 198) admits: 'That multiplier is an honest BOUND, not a calibration: nothing here has yet been measured against realized closes.' So the member UI DOES still render a confidence-labeled percentage that is not backed by any calibration table (it's bounded, not measured against outcomes) — it is just a different field/model than the one #2827 addressed.

This module changes only what the MODEL sees.'); git show 68d7a195f commit message ('The member UI's "conviction" label will be relabeled separately (does not affect model visibility)'). grep -rn -i confidence src/features/spx/lib/spx-play-engine.ts → 0 matches (field fully removed/renamed from the play engine, now called `rawScore`, rendered only as '· {n} Score' text at spx-signals.ts:797, never as a %). grep -rn -i confidence src/features/spx/components/*.tsx → SpxPinForecast.tsx:80,154 (Pin confidence card + aria-label) is the ONLY confidence surface left in the member UI. spx-pin-forecast-core.ts:194-199: 'Anchoring the ceiling to the implied distribution ... **CQ-016** | PROVEN
A: 'Session closed' takes precedence over 'GEX/desk-data-stale' in the rendered action text, and direction does NOT freeze or zero out. Order of block-pushing in `evaluatePlayGates()`: 'Session closed — no new entries' is pushed at line 175 (first, right after the market_open check), while 'Desk data stale (Ns)' — the literal message this codebase uses for what CQ-016 calls 'GEX stale' (there is no exact string 'GEX stale'; the closest is 'GEX walls required — no entry without dealer map' or 'Desk data stale', both pushed later) is pushed at line 304, after Session closed. The rendered gateLine consumer (`spx-play-verdict-bar.ts:198-201`) picks `play.gates.blocks[0]` when gates fail — array order = push order — so 'Session closed' wins as the FIRST block and is what the verdict bar shows. Separately, `firstGateBlockCategory()` (playbook-gate-categories.ts:87-102) classifies 'Session closed' as category 'operational' (matches `/^Session closed/i`) while 'Desk data stale'/GEX messages fall through to the default 'quality' category; GATE_LAYER_ORDER checks operational BEFORE quality, so operational (Session closed) always wins the category-level precedence too. Direction: `playDirection = play.direction ?? play.open_play?.direction ?? null` (spx-play-verdict-bar.ts:235) and every emission site in spx-play-engine.ts sets `direction: dir` (= confluence.direction) independent of `gates.blocks` — direction is computed upstream of gate evaluation and is never zeroed or frozen by any gate block; it continues to reflect the live confluence read even while blocked.
EVIDENCE: spx-play-gates.ts:174-176 ('Session closed — no new entries' pushed first) vs :303-304/:280-281 (Desk-data-stale/GEX-walls pushed later). playbook-gate-categories.ts:22-36 (Session closed → operational regex) and :87-102 (GATE_LAYER_ORDER = [operational, playbook_validity, risk, quality], first non-empty layer wins). spx-play-verdict-bar.ts:198-201 (`gateLine = !play.gates.passed && play.gates.blocks[0] ? play.gates.blocks[0] : ...`) and :235 (`playDirection = play.direction ?? play.open_play?.direction ?? null`, sourced independently of gates). spx-play-engine.ts: consistently `direction: dir` at every emission site (grep found ~15 occurrences, e.g. lines 274,360,369,1062,1185,1212) regardless of whether blocks are populated.

**CQ-017** | PROVEN
A: `/api/market/spx/pulse/stream` re-checks auth ONLY at connection time, not per-tick. `authorizeMarketDeskApi(req)` is called once at the top of the `GET` handler (route.ts:79) before the SSE `ReadableStream` is constructed; the per-connection `send()` function that runs every 250ms (interval at line 171) and the shared 250ms module-level refresher never call any auth function again — they only read the shared `latestSnapshot`/store objects. `authorizeMarketDeskApi` gates at 'community' tier (`market-api-auth.ts:83-86`), the lowest paid tier, so the leak window if a viewer's tier is downgraded (or session revoked) mid-stream is the full remaining lifetime of that SSE connection — bounded only by the client disconnecting, an idle-timeout heartbeat failure (15s heartbeat interval, line 176-185), or the 2000-connection cap, not by any re-authorization. This is architecturally the SAME pattern as CQ-003's downgrade-leak concern, but the blast radius here is wider because the gate is community-tier (cheaper/most populous cohort) rather than premium-only.
EVIDENCE: route.ts:78-80 (single `authorizeMarketDeskApi(req)` call before stream setup, no re-check inside `send()` at lines 106-164, the 250ms `interval = setInterval(send, 250)` at line 171, or the 15s heartbeat at 176-185). market-api-auth.ts:83-86 (`authorizeMarketDeskApi` = `authorizeCronOrTierApi(req, "community")`, doc comment: 'for the community-tier SPX dashboard's data ... must NOT be used for premium-exclusive products').

The desk payload carries this so the UI can badge estimated readings'). spx-desk.ts:968-972,1753,2096 (field definition + assignment sites). git show 47daa372c --stat (PR #3167 diff) touches only `SpxPlayVerdictBar.tsx`; `git show 47daa372c -- SpxPlayVerdictBar.tsx | grep -in 'internal\|estimated'` → 0 matches. `grep -rln internals_estimated src --include='*.tsx'` → 0 results (no component reads the field at all).

**CQ-019** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: Power-hour is NOT derived from the same fast lane the pulse SSE stream uses. `/api/market/spx/power-hour` calls `loadMergedSpxDesk()` (spx-desk-loader.ts), which is cached under the `spx-merged:${date}` key with a 10s TTL (`deskCacheTtlMs()`, staleWhileRevalidate) — a completely separate cache from `spx:pulse:snapshot` (the Redis key / in-memory `indexStore` the pulse SSE stream refreshes every 250ms and reads as near-real-time). So the two CAN diverge by up to the merged-desk cache's staleness window (10s TTL + whatever SWR staleness the server allows past that) while the pulse lane stays sub-second fresh. However, the power-hour engine's own 'is it power hour right now' gate (`isPowerHourWindow(now)`) is computed from the SERVER CLOCK directly, not from a stale `market_open`/session flag baked into the desk snapshot — so the specific failure mode in the question ('power-hour active while desk says session closed') is less likely than a stale PRICE/GEX figure inside an active power-hour window, since the window-open/closed determination itself doesn't depend on the stale snapshot.
EVIDENCE: src/app/api/market/spx/power-hour/route.ts:6,21-23 (`readSpxPowerHourSnapshot(merged)` where `merged` = `await loadMergedSpxDesk()`). spx-desk-loader.ts:144-165 (`loadMergedSpxDeskCore`→`withServerCache('spx-merged:${date}', deskCacheTtlMs(), ...)`); config.ts:23-28 (`deskCacheTtlMs()` = 10s default). pulse/stream/route.ts:24,32-55 (`latestSnapshot`/`spx:pulse:snapshot` refreshed every 250ms, `isWsUpdatedAtFresh(fresh, 10_000)` 10s freshness check on the in-memory indexStore). spx-power-hour-engine.ts:354,564 (`isPowerHourWindow(now)` — server-time-based, not desk-flag-based).

**CQ-020** | DISPROVEN
A: The premise conflates two different products/tables. `isZeroDteWin` (record.ts) and `labelFromPlanOutcome` (feature-store.ts) belong to the Night Hawk 0DTE system's `zerodte_setup_log` ledger, NOT to SPX Slayer's `spx_play_outcomes` table — `grep`ing every file that references `spx_play_outcomes` for `isZeroDteWin`/`labelFromPlanOutcome` finds only `src/lib/db.ts`, which is a shared DB module containing both systems' unrelated code, not a join between them. `spx_play_outcomes` has its own single writer-side classifier, `classifyOutcome()` in spx-play-outcomes.ts:173, which stamps `outcome` ('win'/'loss'/'breakeven') once at close time — there is no second, independently-computed read-side predicate for SPX rows that could disagree with it the way the two zerodte functions once could. Separately: the Aug-5 4-row disagreement the question references (MU/SPXW/META/OKLO) was a Night Hawk finding and has ALREADY been fixed — `feature-store.ts`'s `labelFromPlanOutcome` now explicitly DELEGATES to `record.ts`'s `isZeroDteWin` (line 65: `return isZeroDteWin(row) ? "win" : "loss"`) rather than re-deriving the label from raw mid columns, per its own doc comment: 'The FIX: call the SAME shared pure predicate ... so this can no longer drift out of sync.'
EVIDENCE: feature-store.ts:20,33-70 (delegation to isZeroDteWin, doc comment naming the historical '4/130 real rows disagreed' bug and its fix). evidence-eval.ts:55-56 ('labelFromPlanOutcome and record.ts's isZeroDteWin were two implementations that could disagree. They are no longer.'). `grep -rln spx_play_outcomes src | xargs grep -ln 'isZeroDteWin|labelFromPlanOutcome'` → only src/lib/db.ts (a shared, unrelated-code file — confirmed by inspecting its spx_play_outcomes-specific lines, none of which reference either function). spx-play-outcomes.ts:173 (`classifyOutcome`) is the sole win/loss/breakeven classifier for that table.

**CQ-021** | PROVEN
A: Nothing in the code prevents a lotto candidate from carrying a direction that conflicts with the main SPX play board's direction. The lotto engine (`spx-lotto-engine.ts`) is explicitly documented as a 'Parallel pre-market lotto track — does NOT affect the main SPX play state machine' (line 443-444 comment). `tryNewWatch()` (lines 426-441) gates a new lotto watch ONLY on confluence SCORE MAGNITUDE — `if (confluence && Math.abs(confluence.score) < minScore) return null` (line 436) — never on whether `catalyst.direction` (the lotto's own direction, sourced independently) agrees in sign with `confluence.direction` (the main play's direction). `evaluateLottoCatalysts()` (spx-lotto-catalyst.ts) derives its own direction purely from flow skew, gap%, and macro catalysts — no reference to the main confluence/play direction anywhere in that function. So a strongly-negative (short-biased) main confluence score with, say, a bullish premarket gap catalyst could produce a long lotto watch displayed alongside a short main play, with nothing in the codebase blocking or even flagging that combination as a conflict (the only 'reversal' concept found, `is_reversal`, tracks a lotto watch reversing relative to ITS OWN prior watch, not relative to the main play).
EVIDENCE: spx-lotto-engine.ts:426-441 (`tryNewWatch`, magnitude-only gate at line 436, no direction-agreement check), :443-444 (parallel-track doc comment). spx-lotto-catalyst.ts:208-238+ (`evaluateLottoCatalysts` builds direction from flow/gap/macro signals with no reference to confluence.direction). spx-lotto-engine.ts:144-165,608-612 (`is_reversal` semantics — a watch's own prior-vs-new direction flip, not cross-checked against the main play).

**CQ-023** | DISPROVEN
A: Night Hawk's `spx_slayer_badge` calls the SAME `buildSpxDesk()`-derived pipeline SPX Slayer itself uses — it is NOT a forked scorer. `zerodte-service.ts:708-710` dynamically imports `spx-slayer-badge.ts`'s `getSpxSlayerBadgeSnapshot()`, which (spx-slayer-badge.ts:24,26,41,53) imports `loadMergedSpxDesk` from `spx-desk-loader.ts` (the identical `spx-merged:${date}` cache lane the SPX desk route itself reads) and `getSpxPlaySnapshot` from `spx-play-engine.ts` — the exact same play-engine function the SPX Slayer desk's own play state uses (confirmed: `getSpxPlaySnapshot` is defined once in spx-play-engine.ts:215 and imported by spx-slayer-badge.ts and nowhere else has a competing reimplementation). Import graph confirmed via grep — no second/forked scorer file found.

**CQ-024** | PROVEN
A: After PR #3937, a future-timestamped GEX `at`/`asof` is neither rejected nor silently clamped to fresh — it is CLAMPED-AWAY-FROM (the old bug) no longer, and now correctly FLOWS THROUGH as a large negative age into the existing fail-closed staleness guard, which flags it STALE. The pre-fix bug: `gexDataAgeMs()` computed `Math.max(0, now - lastGoodGexComputedAt)`, so a clock-skewed future `asof` produced a negative raw age that got clamped to `0` — read by `gexStaleFromAge(0)` as maximally FRESH (the opposite of correct). The fix (commit 1eeb1c277, PR #3937) removed both `Math.max(0, ...)` clamps (in `gexDataAgeMs()` and in the canonical desk GEX path's `gexAgeMs` computation), so the raw (possibly negative) age now reaches `gexStaleFromAge()` unclamped. `gexStaleFromAge(ageMs)` (spx-desk-numerics.ts:28-32) explicitly fail-closes: `if (ageMs < -WS_TIMESTAMP_FUTURE_TOLERANCE_MS) return true` (stale) — with a 5-second tolerance (`WS_TIMESTAMP_FUTURE_TOLERANCE_MS = 5_000`) for ordinary clock skew before it trips. So production behavior for a future-`at` GEX cache entry more than 5s ahead of the request time: served WITH a `gex_stale: true` flag (not rejected outright, not silently served fresh).
EVIDENCE: git show 1eeb1c277 (PR #3937) diff on spx-desk.ts: removed `Math.max(0, now - lastGoodGexComputedAt)` → `now - lastGoodGexComputedAt`; removed `Math.max(0, Date.now() - asofMs)` → `Date.now() - asofMs`, commit message: 'Math.max(0, now - asof) converted clock-skewed future pos.asof into gex_age_ms=0, which gexStaleFromAge treats as fresh. Remove the clamp ... so negative age reaches the existing fail-closed guard.' spx-desk-numerics.ts:14-32 (`GEX_STALE_MS` default 30s; `gexStaleFromAge`: `if (ageMs == null) return true; if (ageMs < -WS_TIMESTAMP_FUTURE_TOLERANCE_MS) return true;`). timestamp-freshness.ts:2 (`WS_TIMESTAMP_FUTURE_TOLERANCE_MS = 5_000`). Regression test spx-desk-gex-age-freshness.test.ts (asserts the clamp is gone and the raw-age path feeds `gex_stale: gexStaleFromAge(gexAgeMs)`) — added in the same PR, proving RED→GREEN discipline was followed.

## C. HELIX

**CQ-025** | PROVEN
A: No dedicated alert fires. flow-liveness.ts (src/lib/flow-liveness.ts) is a pure Redis-heartbeat read/write module with three read APIs (isFlowFrameFreshFromCluster, isFlowFrameFreshAnywhere, peekFlowLivenessHeartbeat) and one writer (markFlowFrameDelivered). None of them call any notification path — grepping the whole file and its only consumer for observability (src/lib/admin-helix-health.ts) turns up zero notifyOpsDiscord/PagerDuty/CloudWatch-alarm calls. The ONLY place the heartbeat is surfaced is admin-helix-health.ts's 'Leg 2' (lines ~155-167), which calls peekFlowLivenessHeartbeat() purely to populate a passive admin-dashboard field (`tape.heartbeat_present`/`fresh`) that a human has to open the BIE admin panel to see — the module's own header explicitly says 'this panel must never add upstream cost just from being viewed,' i.e. it is read-on-demand, not push-alerted. Contrast with uw-rate-limiter.ts's `alertRedisDegradedOnce()` (same file family), which DOES page ops via `notifyOpsDiscord` on a degraded-state transition — flow-liveness.ts has no analogous once-latch alert for 'WS heartbeat went stale while a cron kept running REST.' So today: a WS-holder replica dying while cron backfill masks it is visible ONLY in (a) the admin dashboard if someone looks, and (b) console.warn/CloudWatch Logs lines from flow-ingest.ts's own cron-skip logic — there is no automated alert/dashboard-name wired to fire on this specific divergence.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/flow-liveness.ts (whole file, no alert call); src/lib/admin-helix-health.ts:22-49 (module header, 'Leg 2' doc) and lines 155-167 (peekFlowLivenessHeartbeat call, passive panel read); contrast src/lib/providers/uw-rate-limiter.ts:198-224 (`alertRedisDegradedOnce` -> `notifyOpsDiscord`, the pattern that IS wired for a different degraded condition).

**CQ-026** | PROVEN
A: The dedupe key is `alert_id`, enforced as a real Postgres uniqueness constraint via `ON CONFLICT (alert_id) DO NOTHING ... RETURNING id` in `insertFlowAlert` (src/lib/db.ts). `alertId()` (src/lib/flow-persist.ts:66-70) builds this key as `uw:${row.id ?? row.alert_id}` when UW supplies an upstream id, falling back to a widened composite key from `flowFallbackAlertId()` (src/lib/flow-alert-id.ts: `uw:${ticker}:${alerted_at}:${strike}:${option_type}:${premium}[:${trade_count}]`) only when UW sends no id at all. Both the WS path (src/lib/ws/uw-socket.ts multiple call sites) and the REST cron path (src/lib/providers/flow-ingest.ts:108) funnel through the SAME `persistAndPublishFlowAlert()` function, which computes this SAME id and passes it to the SAME `insertFlowAlert` ON CONFLICT clause — so if both deliver the identical UW print within 5 seconds, the second INSERT's `rowCount` is 0 (`inserted=false`) and `shouldFanOut()` suppresses the duplicate SSE/Discord publish (flow-persist.ts:224-231, explicit comment: 'stops the WS+REST double-post of the same whale'). Genuine ON-CONFLICT duplicates therefore cannot double-insert into flow_alerts.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/db.ts insertFlowAlert(): `ON CONFLICT (alert_id) DO NOTHING RETURNING id`; src/lib/flow-persist.ts:66-70 (alertId), :159-231 (persistAndPublishFlowAlert, shouldFanOut gating); src/lib/flow-alert-id.ts (flowFallbackAlertId); both src/lib/ws/uw-socket.ts and src/lib/providers/flow-ingest.ts:108 call persistAndPublishFlowAlert.

**CQ-027** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: Static-only answer for the sort key (proven); the neutral-aggressor ranking question needs a live/data check. Neither `/api/market/flows/route.ts` (REST) nor the underlying `readFlowsMemberCached`/`buildFlowsMemberPayload` pipeline sorts by any 'bullish/neutral/bearish' classification at all — the REST route has no explicit `ORDER BY`; ordering is done client-side in `displayAlerts` (src/features/helix/components/FlowFeed.tsx, ~line 888): `[...filteredTapeBuffer].sort((a,b) => flowTimeMs(b) - flowTimeMs(a))`, i.e. a pure newest-first TIME sort — there is no premium/volume/OI-delta secondary key at all, and 'aggressor' (bullish/bearish/neutral, computed from `ask_pct` via `flowDirection`/`aggressorRead` elsewhere in the codebase) plays NO role in default tape ordering. So a block trade classified `neutral` aggressor is NOT specially excluded or demoted from the default view — it simply appears in its time slot alongside directional prints, indistinguishable in ranking. What I could not verify from code alone: whether the UI's default FILTER state (as opposed to sort) pre-selects a 'bullish only' view that would hide neutral prints — that lives in FlowFeed's filter-state initialization, which I did not fully trace in this pass.
EVIDENCE: src/app/api/market/flows/route.ts (no ORDER BY in the route itself); src/features/helix/components/FlowFeed.tsx ~line 888 (displayAlerts sort, pure time-desc via flowTimeMs, no aggressor/premium/OI key). LIVE CHECK to close the gap: inspect FlowFeed's initial filter-panel state (default side/aggressor filter, if any) and confirm via `curl` on `/api/market/flows` that neutral-aggressor rows are present unfiltered in a live response.

**CQ-028** | DISPROVEN
A: The premise of an asymmetry is false — LAUNCHED_TOOLS cannot block flows on EITHER surface, and in fact neither the page nor the API even checks the tool-launch system for flows today. `src/lib/tool-access.ts` registers `flows` with `defaultLaunched: true` (line ~31), and `isToolLaunched()` (line 69) is `meta.defaultLaunched || envLaunchedKeys(env).has(key)` — a default-launched tool is `true` unconditionally regardless of LAUNCHED_TOOLS (the module's own header states this explicitly: 'default-launched tools cannot be locked via env'). Separately, and more importantly: `src/app/(site)/flows/layout.tsx` calls only `requireTier("premium")`, and `src/app/api/market/flows/route.ts` / `stream/route.ts` call only `authorizePremiumDeskApi()` (which resolves to `authorizeCronOrTierApi(req, "premium")` in src/lib/market-api-auth.ts) — neither route imports or calls `requireToolApi`/`requireToolApiForDeskCaller`/`canAccessTool` from tool-access-server.ts at all. (Contrast with the HELIX alert-rules CRUD route, src/app/api/helix/alerts/rules/route.ts, which DOES call `requireToolApi("flows")` in addition to tier.) So there is no scenario where LAUNCHED_TOOLS blocks the flows PAGE or the flows DATA API differently — both are ungated by the tool-launch system today; only the alert-rules sub-feature is tool-gated.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/tool-access.ts:31 (`defaultLaunched: true` for flows), :69 (isToolLaunched logic), header comment 'default-launched tools cannot be locked via env'; src/app/(site)/flows/layout.tsx (requireTier only); src/app/api/market/flows/route.ts and stream/route.ts (authorizePremiumDeskApi only, no requireToolApi call); src/lib/market-api-auth.ts (authorizePremiumDeskApi -> authorizeCronOrTierApi tier-only); counter-example src/app/api/helix/alerts/rules/route.ts:29 (does call requireToolApi("flows")).

**CQ-029** | PROVEN
A: Ordering uses the REAL UW event time (functionally 'executedAt'), never a local receivedAt/ingest wall-clock, and a genuinely late-arriving backfilled print does NOT jump to the top of the live tape. In src/lib/flow-persist.ts (persistAndPublishFlowAlert), `event.alerted_at = realCreatedAt ?? ""` where `realCreatedAt` is parsed from the UW payload's own `created_at`/`start_time` fields (lines ~161-171) — explicitly NOT the ingest/receive time, per the in-code comment: 'Store the REAL UW alert time only ... Do NOT fall back to flow.alerted_at.' The client-side merge/sort in src/features/helix/lib/helix-flow-tape-merge.ts (`flowTimeSortKey`, line 41-45) and FlowFeed.tsx's `displayAlerts` sort both order strictly by this `alerted_at` value, newest-first — a print whose real UW timestamp is 45 minutes old sorts to its true chronological position, 45 minutes back, regardless of when the SSE event physically arrived. There is no separate 'arrival order'/insertion-order highlighting path in FlowFeed.tsx (grepped for isNew/flash/highlight — none found for flow rows). The one edge case the code documents and fixes: an UNDATED row (UW sent no timestamp at all) is placed at the TOP of a head-page merge and the BOTTOM of an older-page append (by design, since neither position can be 'correct') — but that is the undated case, not the late-but-dated case this question asks about. Staleness badges (FlowFeed.tsx ~line 905-930, `dataStale`) are computed from the newest VALID `alerted_at` across the buffer, so a late-arriving OLD print cannot make the tape read falsely LIVE either.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/flow-persist.ts:159-183 (realCreatedAt derivation + explicit 'Do NOT fall back' comment) and :219 (`event.alerted_at = realCreatedAt ?? ""`); src/features/helix/lib/helix-flow-tape-merge.ts:41-45 (flowTimeSortKey uses alerted_at only), :52-93 (byTimeDesc sort, undated-row placement documented as the one deliberate exception); src/features/helix/components/FlowFeed.tsx ~888 (displayAlerts sort) and ~897-930 (newestAt/dataStale derivation, excludes future-skewed/undated rows via signalWindowAgeMs).

**CQ-030** | DISPROVEN
A: HELIX does not compute an 'OI delta' (change) at all — the premise that it displays a computed change is false. `extractChainFieldsFromRaw()` (src/lib/flow-raw-fields.ts:90) reads `open_interest` as a raw pass-through: `numFromRaw(raw, "open_interest", "oi")` — whatever single snapshot value UW's own flow-alert payload reports at print time, with zero app-side arithmetic against a prior value. This value is used downstream only as a STATIC comparison basis for position-intent heuristics in src/features/helix/lib/helix-position-intent.ts (opening vs closing: is contract size > reported OI?), and is rendered as a single number in ContractDrilldownDrawer.tsx and HelixFlowTable.tsx ("Open int." column) — never as a delta/change field, and no 'oi_change'/'oiDelta' symbol exists anywhere in src/features/helix (grepped, zero hits). Consequently the Monday-open question doesn't apply the way it's posed: there is no app-side 'prior-day vs current' OI computation to go wrong on a Monday — whatever OI value is wrong or stale on a Monday open would be a fact about UW's own upstream OI snapshot (last update time on their side), not anything this codebase derives or caches across sessions.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/flow-raw-fields.ts:90 (open_interest = numFromRaw(raw, 'open_interest', 'oi'), pure snapshot passthrough); src/features/helix/lib/helix-position-intent.ts:94-116 (uses open_interest as a single static comparator, e.g. 'no_open_interest'/'exceeds_open_interest', never a delta); src/features/helix/components/ContractDrilldownDrawer.tsx:286,372-375 and HelixFlowTable.tsx:248 (render the raw value); zero matches for oi_change/oiDelta/oi_delta across src/features/helix.

**CQ-031** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: No per-request-attributed 'percentage of member-initiated flow refreshes that hit 429/throttle' metric exists — only aggregate, non-attributed observability. src/lib/providers/uw-rate-limiter.ts exposes `uwRateLimiterStats()` (line 651), consumed by src/lib/admin-health.ts and src/lib/bie/diagnostic.ts, which reports point-in-time snapshot fields (`tokens`, `inFlight`, `recent429s` in the last 60s, `degraded`, `circuitOpen`) — this is a CLUSTER-WIDE gauge, not a per-caller/per-member breakdown, so it cannot answer 'what % of member-initiated flow refreshes' specifically (it doesn't distinguish a member's `/flows` poll from a background cron's UW call). The only per-event signal is the log line `formatQueueWaitLog()` (line 553) — `[uw] queue wait {ms}ms (background sweep)` — written only when a wait exceeds 500ms, and `noteUw429`'s `[uw] {N} rate-limited endpoints in last 60s` summary (line 494-501) — both are console.warn lines in CloudWatch Logs, not a dashboard metric filter or CloudWatch alarm (no grep hit for a metric-filter definition tied to these strings in this repo). I could not find `flows` REST/SSE routes explicitly opting the caller path into `runWithBackgroundUwSweep` tagging, so a member's own /flows fetch competes on the FULL ceiling (2 RPS cluster-wide, MIN_SPACING_MS=300ms) alongside every other UW consumer — under RTH load with many concurrent premium members, throttling is architecturally plausible but I have no measured percentage.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/providers/uw-rate-limiter.ts:651-691 (uwRateLimiterStats, cluster-wide gauge only) consumed by src/lib/admin-health.ts and src/lib/bie/diagnostic.ts; :553-561 (formatQueueWaitLog, per-call log only above 500ms); :494-501 (noteUw429/maybeFlushRateLimitSummary, 60s aggregate warn). LIVE CHECK to prove a real percentage: during RTH, hit `/api/admin/health` (or wherever uwRateLimiterStats is surfaced) repeatedly while polling `/api/market/flows`, and separately grep CloudWatch Logs `/ecs/blackout-production` for `[uw] queue wait` / `rate-limited endpoints` density over a fixed window to derive an actual throttle rate — no such run was performed in this pass.

**CQ-032** | DISPROVEN
A: Alert rules cannot exfiltrate data this way — they are strictly per-user scoped for CRUD, and delivery goes ONLY to the rule's own owning member's Web Push subscription, never to Discord/a webhook/any shared channel. `src/app/api/helix/alerts/rules/route.ts`'s GET/PUT/DELETE all resolve `userId` from Clerk `requireTierApi("premium")` (via `requireHelixAlertAuth()`, line 26-31) and every SQL statement filters `WHERE user_id = $1` (GET line 51/62, PUT's upsert keyed on `ON CONFLICT (user_id, ticker)` line 100, DELETE line 128) against the table's own composite PK `(user_id, ticker)` (helix-alert-rules-db.ts line 39) — there is no route parameter that lets a caller name another user's rows, so cross-user read/write (IDOR) is not reachable through this API. Delivery is separately confirmed non-Discord/non-webhook: `notifyHelixAlertSubscribers()` (src/lib/helix-alert-notify.ts) is gated on `HELIX_ALERTS_PUSH=1` + VAPID config and calls `sendWebPush()` — a per-subscriber Web Push notification to that SAME `row.user_id`'s own registered device, not a broadcast. The UNRELATED community Discord fan-out (`notifyDiscord` in flow-persist.ts, gated on premium >=$500K threshold) is a fixed, code-defined rule for ALL qualifying prints — members cannot configure or redirect it via the alert-rules CRUD at all.
EVIDENCE: src/app/api/helix/alerts/rules/route.ts:26-31 (requireHelixAlertAuth: tier + tool gate, userId from Clerk auth), :51-62 (GET WHERE user_id=$1), :100 (PUT upsert ON CONFLICT (user_id,ticker)), :128 (DELETE WHERE user_id=$1 AND ticker=$2); src/features/helix/lib/helix-alert-rules-db.ts:39 (PRIMARY KEY (user_id, ticker)); src/lib/helix-alert-notify.ts:1-60 (per-user sendWebPush, HELIX_ALERTS_PUSH gate, cooldown keyed by row.user_id); contrast src/lib/flow-persist.ts notifyDiscord (unconfigurable, code-fixed $500K/route='whale' Discord fan-out, unrelated to member rules).

**CQ-033** | PROVEN
A: The SSE route never emits an explicit 'stale' event and never closes the stream on an upstream (UW) disconnect — it is entirely passive to that condition. `src/app/api/market/flows/stream/route.ts` sends only two event shapes: `{type:'connected'}` once at open, and `{type:'heartbeat', ts}` every 25s on a fixed interval (line ~108-110) — a pure keep-alive independent of whether the UW upstream socket is delivering frames. There is no branch anywhere in this route that inspects UW/WS connection health and no code path that publishes a `{type:'stale'}` event or calls `controller.close()` on upstream silence; the ONLY close paths are: (a) the CLIENT disconnecting (`cancel()` -> `cleanup()`), (b) per-request entitlement re-check (`recheckSseUserEntitlement`) returning 'forbidden' -> emits an `event: error` then closes, and (c) backpressure (`sseBackpressureExceeded`) dropping a slow client. Meanwhile the underlying UW multiplex socket (src/lib/ws/uw-socket.ts) manages its OWN reconnect independently via `reconnectIfStalled()`/`scheduleReconnect` with exponential backoff (1s up to 30s) plus a WS-21 REST-backfill reconciliation on reconnect — none of that state is surfaced to the SSE client. So on the client side (FlowFeed.tsx), a dead upstream simply means `subscribeFlowEvents` stops firing 'flow' callbacks while heartbeats keep arriving every 25s (connection reads healthy); the client independently infers staleness from the AGE of the last real print's `alerted_at` via a local 5-minute threshold (`dataStale`, FlowFeed.tsx ~line 930) driven by its own 10s re-render timer, NOT from anything the server pushes. This directly disproves the 'server emits a stale event' half of the question and confirms the other: reconnect semantics for the upstream provider are entirely server-internal and invisible to SSE clients; staleness detection is 100% client-side inference off real print timestamps.
EVIDENCE: src/app/api/market/flows/stream/route.ts (full file): heartbeat interval line ~108-110 (`{type:'heartbeat'}` every 25_000ms, unconditional); cleanup/close paths: cancel() (client disconnect), recheckSseUserEntitlement 'forbidden' branch (entitlement-only close), sseBackpressureExceeded (slow-client close) — no upstream-health branch anywhere; src/lib/ws/uw-socket.ts:637-676 (reconnectIfStalled, scheduleReconnect backoff) and :1233-1320 (WS-21 reconnect-gap reconciliation) — entirely server-internal, no signal surfaced to the SSE layer; src/features/helix/components/FlowFeed.tsx ~897-930 (client-side dataStale computed from newestAt + 10s ageTick timer, 5-min threshold, no server push consumed for staleness).

Checked directly against the doc's file lists: #3 DTE+IV Context Row -> src/features/helix/components/HelixContextHeader.tsx EXISTS (and reads open_interest per CQ-030 above); #4 Export to CSV -> src/features/helix/lib/helix-csv-export.ts EXISTS; #5 Execution Analysis (Ask%) -> HelixFlowTable.tsx renders `askPctTone`/`fmtAskPct`/`executionQualityHint` off `flow.ask_pct` (lines 235-241) EXISTS; #6 Session Correlation Matrix -> src/features/helix/components/SessionCorrelationMatrix.tsx + helix-session-correlation.ts EXIST; #7 Flow Clustering -> src/features/helix/lib/helix-flow-clusters.ts EXISTS; #8 Volume Profile Overlay -> src/features/helix/components/StrikeVolumeProfile.tsx EXISTS. ## D. Thermal

**CQ-035** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: For SPX, gamma uses the CASH INDEX (Polygon I:SPX), never an ES-futures proxy. There is NO code handling cash/futures divergence because futures are never referenced at all in this pipeline.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/providers/polygon-options-gex.ts:87 `const underlyingRoot = root === "SPX" ? "I:SPX" : root;` then :91 `const snap = await resolveSpotSnapshot(underlyingRoot);`. resolveSpotSnapshot (lines 1294-1319) for `I:` roots tries live WS index tick first (liveWsIndexSpot, fresh within GEX_INDEX_WS_STALE_MS=120s default), then REST `/v3/snapshot/indices`, then resolveSpotSnapshotLastResort (lines 1275-1291) which for I:SPX uses `SPY prev-close * 10` as a last resort ('tracks within ~0.4%'). `grep -n "ES=F|futures|CME|E-mini"` across polygon-options-gex.ts and spx-*.ts returns zero hits — no futures price is ever fetched. Live check that would fully close this: compare /api/market/gex-heatmap?ticker=SPX spot/spot_source against a live SPX cash print during RTH to confirm no futures value ever surfaces.

**CQ-036** | PROVEN
A: VEX, DEX and CHARM are computed/served in the SAME single pass as GEX — not separate endpoints/caches — so they cannot individually go stale relative to GEX. Only the header SPOT is fetched on a separate cadence from the matrix.
EVIDENCE: src/features/thermal/components/GexHeatmap.tsx:231-279 documents 'Restructured payload from /api/market/gex-heatmap: shared axes + gex/vex blocks' with gex/vex?/dex?/charm? all in one type sharing one strikes/expiries axis. Lines 2627-2628 build ONE URL (`/api/market/gex-heatmap?ticker=...`) fetched via a single useSWR at line 2649 (`refreshInterval: matrixPollMs`) — no second SWR for VEX/DEX/CHARM. A separate useSWR at line 2674 (`refreshInterval: quotePollMs`) polls only the header quote/spot, which is the one thing deliberately decoupled (see CQ-038); overlays (dark-pool/flow) are also separately-cadenced per the comment at line 319 ('dark-pool source under it up to ~2min ... can be staler than the matrix') but VEX/DEX/CHARM are not.

**CQ-037** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: 'King node' = argmax |net dealer gamma| strike (absolute value) — same formula shared by Largo and the desk UI, but applied to different SCOPES (multi-expiry aggregate vs a single drilled-down expiry) depending on the surface.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/providers/gex-cross-validation-core.ts:93-107 kingFromStrikeTotals: tracks maxAbs=Math.abs(g) over strike totals. src/lib/gex-king-node-labels.ts:1-6 confirms: 'Argmax |net dealer gamma| strike... same on SPX Slayer and BlackOut Thermal (desk gex_king, matrix anchor, KeyLevelBox)'. Largo's gex_king_strike (src/lib/largo/gex-heatmap-for-largo.ts:241) sources from getGexPositioning, which calls the same kingFromStrikeTotals (gex-positioning.ts:439) against the multi-expiry-aggregate gex.strike_totals (summed over ~15 near-term expiries per polygon-options-gex.ts:287-288 comment). But GexHeatmap.tsx (Thermal UI) uses its OWN local anchorStrike() re-implementation (lines 554-571, same argmax-|value| math) applied to whatever totals are passed — which for a drilled-down single-expiry view is client-FILTERED per-expiry totals, not the server aggregate. SpxGexMatrixHeatmap.tsx (SPX Slayer) does import the real kingFromStrikeTotals (line 298) but applies it per-expiry-column via columnTotalsForAxis. So the strike Largo reports (always aggregate) can legitimately differ from what a member sees highlighted in a drilled-down single-expiry Thermal view for the same ticker/instant — a scope mismatch, not a formula mismatch. Live check to close fully: fetch /api/market/gex-heatmap?ticker=X for Largo's king_strike vs the UI's drilled-down king marker at the same instant.

**CQ-038** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: 'Live push' spot is Polygon's stocks WEBSOCKET aggregate-trade feed (per-second A.* bars), relayed server->browser over SSE — not raw client WS, not NBBO quote data. PR #3944 (commit 4433d215c) rebased ThermalTripleDesk header change_pct on this push spot when it diverges from the matrix snapshot.
EVIDENCE: ThermalTripleDesk.tsx TripleColumn uses useLiveQuoteStream([ticker]) (src/hooks/useLiveQuoteStream.ts) which opens an SSE connection via createSpotStreamEventSource to /api/market/stocks/spot-stream; that route's hub (src/lib/ws/stocks-spot-stream-hub.ts) builds each frame from getStockLiveCandle(ticker) reading stock-candle-store, which is fed by the Polygon stocks WS A.* subscription per the file's own header comment. git show 4433d215c: headerChangePct now = `rebaseChangePct(pushSpot, {price: matrixSpot, change_pct: matrixChangePct}) ?? pushChangePct ?? matrixChangePct` when both pushSpot and matrixSpot exist, matching a prior fix to GexHeatmap.tsx's own header ('matching the GexHeatmap header discipline fixed 2026-09-04'). No max-lag-vs-NBBO number exists anywhere in the repo since the source is 1s-cadence trade aggregates, not tick quotes. Live check: capture pushQuote.price/timestamp vs a simultaneous Polygon NBBO quote fetch during a fast RTH move.

**CQ-039** | PROVEN
A: On a force-rebuild that exceeds GEX_HEATMAP_FORCE_MAX_BLOCK_MS (default 55s), the API hands off the LAST-GOOD CACHED matrix (never a partial matrix, never a 503) — but the response carries NO explicit stale/timed-out flag; only the asof timestamp lets a client infer staleness. A doc comment claiming it 'fails closed with no stale handoff' is itself wrong/stale relative to the actual code.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/providers/config.ts:86-92 gexHeatmapForceMaxBlockMs() doc says 'fail[s] closed (no stale handoff)' — but src/lib/providers/polygon-options-gex.ts:1749-1751 (the function that actually implements it, awaitHeatmapBuildWithBlockCap) says the opposite: 'Force waits longer... but still hands off stale on timeout — better than holding the HTTP request until the ALB 504s with no matrix at all.' Implementation at lines 1752-1769: `Promise.race([build..., setTimeout(blockMs, () => resolveHeatmapStaleHandoff(...))])`; resolveHeatmapStaleHandoff (~1735-1747) prefers a within-TTL cache entry, else most-recent-any-age, else null only if there's truly no prior cache. grep across src/app/api/market/gex-heatmap/route.ts found no `stale: true`/`timed_out` boolean anywhere in the response-building code — only `asof` is present, so callers must compute age themselves rather than read an explicit flag.

**CQ-040** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: GEX (gamma) itself is unaffected by the dividend-yield gap because it uses Polygon's PROVIDER-supplied gamma directly, not our closed-form BS. VEX/CHARM's dividend-yield gap (which DOES use closed-form BS) WAS fixed via a /v3/reference/dividends fallback. A separate diagnostic (the synthetic depth ladder) still shows a raw BS-vs-provider gamma gap for QQQ/IWM, which was ACCEPTED and mitigated by anchoring rather than fixed at the formula level.
EVIDENCE: polygon-options-gex.ts lines 3232, 4252, 4279, 4446 all read `c.greeks?.gamma` directly from the Polygon chain response for the shipped GEX matrix — no closed-form gamma function exists for the primary GEX path. resolveHeatmapDividendYieldUncached (lines 1908-1927): '/stocks/financials/v1/ratios covers companies, not funds — it structurally has no row for an ETF (SPY/QQQ/IWM/VOO/DIA all correctly return null here, live-verified 2026-08-28), which is why every SPX/NDX/RUT heatmap... always fell back to q=0'; fix falls back to /v3/reference/dividends trailing-12mo yield, with comment at line 1942 explicitly: 'pinning a blip's q=0 for an hour would silently reinstate the exact 10-22% VEX/CHARM under-read this change exists to fix.' Separately, per the committed audit-toolkit description of gex-depth-validate.mjs (CLAUDE.md), the synthetic depth ladder's closed-form-vs-provider gamma gap measured 2026-08-12 was 9.5% (SPY)/15.8% (QQQ)/21.7% (IWM), attributed to the same dividend-yield gap but at that layer mitigated by anchoring the ladder to the matrix's own gex.total at spot, not fixed at the formula level. Live check: `node --import tsx scripts/audit/gex-depth-validate.mjs --tickers=QQQ,IWM` against production for a current manual recompute.

**CQ-041** | DISPROVEN
A: Nothing mathematically breaks comparing SPX to AAPL: GEX/VEX/DEX/CHARM are all DOLLAR-notional (greek x OI x 100-multiplier x spot), and the 100-contract multiplier applies to every US-listed option regardless of whether the underlying itself trades in discrete shares, so SPX (cash-settled, no shares) computes through the identical formula path as AAPL. Profiles are shown in absolute per-ticker strike space (each column banded around its own spot), not normalized to %-move, which is a presentational limitation, not a math bug.
EVIDENCE: src/features/thermal/lib/thermal-compare-presets.ts includes SPX directly alongside single-name equities (id:'indices' tickers:['SPY','SPX','QQQ','IWM'], id:'mega' tickers include AAPL etc). thermal-compact-matrix.ts:52-77 bandStrikesAroundSpot bands each ticker's own strikes around ITS OWN spot — no shared %-distance-from-spot axis found anywhere in ThermalCompactMatrix.tsx or ThermalCompareStrip.tsx (grep for 'pctFromSpot'/'moveFromSpot'/distance-normalization returns nothing); ThermalCompareStrip.tsx:143-144/167 shows only per-ticker decimal-precision formatting (SPX 1dp, others 2dp), confirming absolute-strike display. No cross-ticker arithmetic combining SPX and AAPL strikes was found in the GEX/VEX/DEX/CHARM aggregation code (polygon-options-gex.ts), which computes per-ticker independently.

GexHeatmap.tsx has ~50 useMemo calls (grep count) and no key={ticker} forced remount (only ThermalTripleDesk.tsx:521 uses key={ticker} for its subcomponents) — a ticker switch changes the SWR fetch key, producing a new data/view object that invalidates most downstream useMemos (filteredTotals, wall/flip recompute, profile curve), functionally forcing a full recompute without an explicit remount. docs/audit/FINDINGS.md line ~28340 documents related but different perf/UX defects already fixed on this component (an 'infinite' CSS pulse animation causing ambient GPU load on extreme cells; a ~200ms hydration skeleton shape-jump) — real evidence of perf attention on this surface, but no p95 grid-render number. Live check needed: a Chrome DevTools/React Profiler trace on production /heatmap for SPX with all expiries selected on comparable hardware, plus adding /heatmap coverage to seo-cwv-monitor.mjs or a dedicated INP harness.

**CQ-043** | PROVEN
A: PR #3134 added an explicit spot-validation guard to recomputeLevels rejecting NaN, +/-Infinity, zero, and negative spot, falling back to null (server-computed) levels. No production metric counts these rejections — it is a silent, client-side, zero-telemetry guard.
EVIDENCE: git show aef71b0a7 (PR #3134) added src/features/thermal/lib/gex-heatmap/recompute-levels.ts:23-26: `if (!Number.isFinite(spot) || spot <= 0) return { posWall: null, negWall: null, flip: null };` with commit message stating: 'a stale or corrupted spot price (NaN, infinite, or <=0) could propagate through the flip calculation and produce invalid levels.' Called from GexHeatmap.tsx:3048 `recomputeLevels(filteredTotals, spot)`. A grep for any rejection counter/metrics emission tied to this guard across observability/metrics files and the function itself found nothing — no production metric exists counting how often this fires per day.

**CQ-044** | PROVEN
A: gex-cross-validation.ts's own doc says the validator 'does NOT block the primary data path and only logs divergences' — Polygon stays the served primary always, never swapped for UW. But downstream, the divergence IS surfaced as an explicit UI warning banner, gated to the GEX lens only (no equivalent for VEX/DEX/CHARM), and only when the near-term-scoped UW WS ladder is actually fresh (off-hours the check silently never runs at all, reading as an 'offline' chip rather than agreement).
EVIDENCE: src/lib/providers/gex-cross-validation.ts lines 3-12 module doc: 'it does NOT block the primary data path and only logs divergences.' src/features/thermal/components/GexHeatmap.tsx:3202-3207 computes `uwDiverged` = true when `lens === 'gex'` AND `uwCross.divergence > 5` AND NOT (callWallMatch && putWallMatch && flipMatch); lines 3778-3783 render an amber banner when true: 'Our two data sources disagree by {N}pt on where the walls sit — treat these levels as provisional until they agree.' The condition explicitly gates on lens==='gex', so VEX/DEX/CHARM have no equivalent warning. PR #3134's commit message (git show aef71b0a7) separately characterizes a second, quieter gate: validateGexAgainstUW needs the UW WS ladder fresh within 120s and, failing that, returns null whenever a near-term expiry scope is supplied (REST fallback cannot be scoped per-expiry) — measured off-hours 2026-08-23: cross_validation was null across 12 reads of SPY/SPX/QQQ/IWM even at 1,600+ seconds of matrix age, confirming the check simply never runs off-hours rather than reporting agreement.

## E. Vector

**CQ-045** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: Yes — a real cadence mismatch exists, though it's the full-state cache (not the vector:universe:snapshot key) that pick-sweep actually reads. `vector-pick-sweep` runs every 2 minutes but reads `fetchVectorFullState`'s cache-first Redis entry, which is only refreshed by the `vector-full-state-snapshot` cron on a ~5-minute schedule (15-minute TTL, sized to tolerate one missed cron cycle). So at least 1-2 of every ~3 pick-sweep runs rank against the SAME full-state snapshot, meaning picks can be built from full-state data up to ~5 minutes stale by design, and up to 15 minutes stale if a single cron cycle is delayed/skipped (which would not itself trigger an alert until stale_after_min:15 fires). It does not reach 10+ minutes under a single missed cycle, but a two-cycle miss (10 min) sits inside the TTL window undetected.
EVIDENCE: cron-registry.ts:442-452 (vector-universe-snapshot, ~5min, stale_after_min 15) and :454-464 (vector-full-state-snapshot, ~5min, stale_after_min 15); vector-pick-sweep/route.ts:14-16 (schedule_cron_utc '1-59/2 11-21 * * 1-5' = every 2min); vector-full-state-cache.ts VECTOR_FULL_STATE_CACHE_TTL_SEC=15*60 and its comment 'comfortably outlive the ~5-min RTH cron cadence'; vector-full-state.ts:364-370 cache-first read; src/lib/vector/vector-pick-sweep.ts imports fetchVectorFullState + vectorUniverseTickers, never the vector:universe:snapshot key directly.

**CQ-046** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: Yes, overlap remains possible: the lock is a Redis SET-NX-with-expiry, not a real mutex, so any sweep exceeding the current 900s TTL lets the next scheduled fire re-acquire the lock and start a second sweep concurrently — this exact scenario already happened in production twice at lower TTLs (480s guard breached by 694s and 645s runs). The most recent hard runtime numbers in-repo are elapsed=693684ms and elapsed=644588ms (2026-09-03); I could not pull a live CloudWatch number for the trailing 7 days from this sandbox. LIVE CHECK: CloudWatch Logs Insights on /ecs/blackout-production filtered to '[cron/vector-pick-sweep] done' + 'elapsed=', `stats max(elapsed) by bin(1h)` over the last 7 days; the cron_job_runs table mentioned in the question is Postgres and unreachable here (raw TCP blocked).
EVIDENCE: vector-pick-sweep/route.ts:13-41 (comment block): 2026-09-01 measured elapsed=301362ms against a 120s schedule with no guard; post-480s-TTL-guard, CloudWatch 2026-09-03 16:50-19:15 UTC showed elapsed=693684ms and elapsed=644588ms, both exceeding the 480s TTL, with log timestamps showing an actual overlap (sweep started ~16:55:38/lock expired ~17:03:38/second sweep started ~17:05:16 while first still running until 17:06:52); TTL raised to 900s on 2026-09-03 in response. sharedCacheSetNx is a Redis SET NX EX (finite-TTL lock), not a true mutex.

**CQ-047** | DISPROVEN
A: The two decision trees are completely independent — same labels ('STILL BUY'), entirely different math and inputs. Vector's function is a live-quote/premium-drift state machine keyed on spot-vs-wall invalidation and % drift from entry mid. Night Hawk swing's function is a discrete setup/entry-state machine keyed on commit gates, multi-day entry-validity deadlines, and cross-session persistence — it never references live premium %, bid/ask spread, or a live spot-vs-level tick buffer at all. The only shared concept is 'is a position already open' (intent/deskCommitted), evaluated over unrelated underlying state.
EVIDENCE: vector-pick-live-status.ts:170-350 evaluateVectorPickLiveStatus (premiumPct drift, isSetupInvalidated spot/wall/gammaFlip checks with 0.15% buffer, spread%, MAX_OPTION_PREMIUM_PER_SHARE cap) vs entry-enterability.ts:101-207 evaluateSwingEntryEnterability (SwingSetupState/SwingEntryState state machine, failingSwingCommitGates, ENTRY_VALIDITY_DAYS deadline, persistenceObserved, aboveFloor). Disjoint input types VectorPickLiveEvalInput vs SwingEntryEnterabilityInput; no shared helper found by grep beyond label formatting.

**CQ-048** | PROVEN
A: RTH gating exists on WRITE only, and even there it's inconsistent: the canonical writer defaults to RTH-required, but the SSE-hub writer explicitly opts out (rthRequired:false). The read route has zero time-of-day gate and returns history:[] identically whether (a) genuinely no wall activity occurred, (b) the RTH write path hasn't run yet, or (c) a query landed after-hours before any cron fired — there is no computedAt/coverage-style field in this route's response to disambiguate, unlike the daily-regime route which does carry a retentionNote/coverage field for this exact reason.
EVIDENCE: wall-history/route.ts:38-86 (GET handler, no isEtCashRth or any time gate on read); vector-wall-write.ts:102-132 writeWallHistorySample (default rthRequired:true, skips with skipped:'outside_rth' when !isEtCashRth()); vector-wall-write.ts:140-160 persistWallSampleDebounced (sse-hub source) explicitly passes rthRequired:false, bypassing the gate.

**CQ-049** | PROVEN
A: Source: UW dark-pool prints, pre-warmed every ~10 min into Redis with a 25-min TTL (2.5x cadence) that discloses staleness via fetchedAt rather than expiring on a knife-edge. On a feed outage the writer explicitly skips overwriting good data with nothing, so a stale-but-honest entry survives until TTL expiry, after which reads fall back to an empty-levels shape indistinguishable from 'genuinely zero qualifying prints.' Ranking is unaffected either way — dark pool is display/evidence-only, confirmed by grep showing zero use of darkPoolLevels in any scoring/ranking function; its only consumer appends a cosmetic 'Dark pool' line to a pick's rationale card. So ranking continues silently with no UI flag when the feed is down, exactly as the question suspects.
EVIDENCE: cron-registry.ts:490-501 (vector-dark-pool-warm, ~10min schedule, stale_after_min 20); vector-dark-pool-cache.ts:18 (TTL_SEC=25*60, comment '2.5x the cadence, tolerating one missed run'); vector-dark-pool-cache.ts:74-100 warmVectorDarkPool (skips the Redis write on fetchFailed so a stale-but-honest entry survives; falls back to {levels:[],fetchedAt:0} once TTL expires); grep across vector-play-engine.ts/vector-contract-picks.ts/vector-pick-sweep-core.ts finds darkPoolLevels used ONLY as a passthrough field, never in scoring; only real consumer is vector-pick-evidence.ts:258 darkPoolNearReference (cosmetic evidence line).

**CQ-050** | PROVEN
A: At cap+1 the server returns a synchronous HTTP 503 ('Too many active streams — try again shortly') before the SSE stream opens — no queueing, no silent drop. Acquisition is atomic (fixed from an earlier check-then-act race). The cap is GLOBAL ONLY — no per-user counter exists anywhere in this code, so one user could in principle consume many of the 2000 slots. Important nuance: totalStreams is a plain in-process module variable, not Redis-backed, so 2000 is actually a PER-ECS-TASK cap; the true fleet-wide ceiling is 2000 × (number of running web tasks), not a single cluster-wide 2000.
EVIDENCE: vector/stream/route.ts:23,43-45 (MAX_STREAMS = SSE_MAX_STREAMS ?? 2000; 503 'Too many active streams' on acquisition failure); vector-stream-hub.ts:29,121-125 (tryAcquireVectorStreamConnection: atomic check-and-increment on a bare in-process `let totalStreams`, not Redis-backed).

**CQ-051** | PROVEN
