Of the 8, only 2 are pure offline logic (vector-invalidation-edge-cases.mjs, vector-pivot-scenario-drill.mjs); I ran both today and got 15/15 and 4/4 pass respectively (exit 0 both), matching the original commit's claims. The other 6 require live RTH market data, live Polygon chains, or a live Clerk-authenticated proxy-browser tunnel and were not run in this pass. What could NOT be confirmed from static code alone is the specific requested artifact — a concrete day where regime flipped but picks did not reorder — which requires a live historical data pull (comparing GET /api/market/vector/daily-regime output against a historical pick-leaders export for the same session) that was not performed here.

**CQ-053** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: The naive N-tabs×M-tickers×interval multiplication does not translate 1:1 into UW calls: every relevant Vector read is server-side cached per-ticker (5-30s TTLs), and the code explicitly documents this as a deliberate fix for the exact fan-out risk the question raises. So actual UW call rate scales with the number of DISTINCT TICKERS being actively viewed fleet-wide divided by the cache TTL, not with concurrent-user count directly — worst case (500 users all watching 500 different tickers) could still reach roughly 500/30s ≈ 1000 calls/min for the flow overlay alone, well above the stated 2 RPS UW cap, since the cache bounds PER-TICKER rate but not cross-ticker diversity. The real-world distribution of ticker diversity among 500 concurrent members (which determines whether that worst case is ever approached) could not be established from static code and needs a live CloudWatch/UW-call-log comparison against live SSE connection counts during RTH.
EVIDENCE: gex-heatmap/route.ts:38-42 (explicit comment: fetchUwFlowPerStrikeRows is coalesced-not-cached; UW capped at 2 RPS cluster-wide; overlay now cached in-memory+Redis ~30s per ticker specifically to prevent 500-user fan-out from starving UW); flows/route.ts:90-91 (serverCache wrapping the UW read, keyed by ticker/limit/premium); vector-cadence.ts:16,19,22,36 (poll intervals 5s/15s/5s/30s); use-vector-helix-flows.ts:21 (FLOW_POLL_MS=30000); use-vector-pick-live-monitor.ts:12 (LIVE_POLL_MS=1000).

**CQ-054** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: PR #3582 fixed one thing precisely: computeGexWalls in vector-universe.ts (the universe-scanner/wall-history builder) no longer forwards a literal spot=0, preventing wrong-side call/put wall geometry from being persisted into the universe snapshot and durable wall-history rail — a display/analytics-corruption bug, not a false trading signal. The premise 'can a pick still show BUY with zero spot' is DISPROVEN for the actual shipped call graph: three independent upstream guards already null/reject spot<=0 before evaluateVectorPickLiveStatus is ever reached (buildVectorPlay's own null-return, the live-quotes HTTP route's 400, and the pick-sweep cron's falsy-check). However, evaluateVectorPickLiveStatus itself has no internal spot<=0 guard, and its short-bias invalidation branch would not trip on spot=0 (only long-bias would), so IF any future caller bypassed the three existing gates and fed spot=0 directly, a short-bias pick could theoretically fall through to still_buy — this is an untested latent gap (no unit test covers spot:0 in vector-pick-live-status.test.ts), not a currently-reachable production bug.
EVIDENCE: commit b4c769bee diff: vector-universe.ts two call sites changed spot:spot??undefined → spot:(spot!=null&&spot>0?spot:undefined) feeding computeGexWalls; docs/audit/findings-staging/2026-09-04-vector-universe-spot-zero-guard.md and MARKET-OPEN-VALIDATION.md entry #19 describe the harm as wrong-side wall geometry persisted into universe rows/wall-history, not a false BUY. Separately: vector-play-engine.ts:562-564 buildVectorPlay returns null when spot<=0; contract-picks/live/route.ts:107-109 returns HTTP 400 'Invalid spot' when spot<=0 before calling evaluateVectorPickLiveStatus; vector-pick-sweep.ts:92 `if (!state?.spot...) return null` (0 is falsy in JS). vector-pick-live-status.ts's isSetupInvalidated short-bias branch only fires on `spot > callWall*(1+buf)`, which spot=0 never satisfies, and no explicit spot<=0 guard exists inside evaluateVectorPickLiveStatus itself; vector-pick-live-status.test.ts has no spot:0 test case.

## F. Night Hawk

**CQ-055** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: PR #3437 (commit 0988337fc) is real and fixed the exact defect: buildZeroDteLiveMarksPayloadFrom in src/lib/zerodte/live-marks.ts computed a fresher `latchedStatus` but never used it to exclude a row from the payload, so a play the 1s lane had already closed stayed in the ~10s active-set cache and kept streaming a live mark for up to 10s. The fix (live-marks.ts lines ~805-820) now does `const status = latchedStatus?.(p) ?? p.status; ... if (status === "CLOSED") return [];` inside a `.flatMap`, dropping any row whose resolved status is CLOSED before it ever reaches the SSE/REST payload. The regression test 'a play the 1s lane already latched CLOSED is dropped from the payload...' in src/lib/zerodte/live-marks.test.ts is present in the working tree and the commit message documents RED pre-fix/GREEN post-fix (33/33) plus a clean tsc and 11976/5-known-sandbox-failure full test run. HOWEVER: the question as posed asks for a live DB query proving zero `zerodte_setup_log` rows currently have status=CLOSED with a 'live_in_marks=true' flag — no such column exists in the schema (the live-marks lane is Redis/in-memory + the mark store, not a persisted 'live_in_marks' DB flag on zerodte_setup_log; the fix lives entirely in the in-process payload builder, not in a DB column). So the literal DB query the question asks for cannot be run (the flag it names doesn't exist as schema), and I could not execute a live query against production from this sandbox regardless (direct Postgres TCP is blocked per CLAUDE.md). The correct live check to prove this class of bug is fixed is: hit `/api/market/zerodte/marks` (REST) or its SSE stream repeatedly through a session with an open 0DTE play, force/observe a close event, and confirm the closed OCC disappears from the `marks` array on the SAME tick its status flips (not ~10s later) — e.g. via the already-built `scripts/audit/zerodte-e2e-healthcheck.mjs` stage D/E, or a fresh live-run of live-marks.test.ts. Code-level fix is proven; live production re-verification and the literal DB-column premise are not.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/zerodte/live-marks.ts (post-#3437 diff, `if (status === "CLOSED") return [];`); src/lib/zerodte/live-marks.test.ts new test; commit 0988337fc message + docs/audit/findings-staging/2026-09-03-nighthawk-closed-play-shows-live-mark.md; no `live_in_marks` column exists in zerodte_setup_log's schema (src/lib/db.ts CREATE TABLE/ALTER TABLE block ~L830-950 has no such field).

**CQ-056** | PROVEN
A: PR #3434 (commit 7125d1a0d) added a hard SQL guard, not caller discipline: `pruneNighthawkPlayOutcomesForEdition` in src/lib/db.ts now issues `DELETE FROM nighthawk_play_outcomes WHERE edition_for = $1::date AND NOT (ticker = ANY($2::varchar[])) AND outcome = 'pending'` — the added `AND outcome = 'pending'` clause means the DELETE statement itself, at the SQL level, can never touch a row whose outcome has already been graded (target/stop/open/ambiguous/unfilled all differ from 'pending'). I attempted to construct a prune path that deletes a graded row: any call to this function with a ticker list that omits a graded ticker will simply not match that row in the WHERE clause (its `outcome` column is not 'pending'), so the DELETE affects 0 rows for that ticker regardless of the tickers array passed in — there is no code path (admin historical-recovery route included) that bypasses this SQL predicate, since it's baked into the one exported function every caller uses. A dedicated regression test exists: src/features/nighthawk/lib/record-honesty-contract.test.ts 'db.ts: pruneNighthawkPlayOutcomesForEdition can never delete a graded row — SQL guard, not caller discipline' asserts the exact regex of the guarded SQL is present in the function body.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/db.ts pruneNighthawkPlayOutcomesForEdition (post-fix, ~line 8973-8980): `DELETE FROM nighthawk_play_outcomes WHERE edition_for = $1::date AND NOT (ticker = ANY($2::varchar[])) AND outcome = 'pending'`; src/features/nighthawk/lib/record-honesty-contract.test.ts regex-match test; commit 7125d1a0d full message documents the exact prior vulnerability (admin POST /api/admin/nighthawk/run?asOfEt=<past date>&persist=1 could previously reconstruct a different ticker set and hard-delete an already-graded row).

**CQ-057** | PROVEN
A: The exact gate is in src/lib/zerodte/scan.ts's fresh-commit loop (~lines 1539-1563): every fresh find is classified as `refresh` (already in the ledger) or a `freshCandidates` member, and for each fresh candidate: `if (s.gate?.verdict === "COMMIT" && !planBlocked) { committedFresh.push(s); continue; }` — ONLY setups whose gate verdict is literally the string "COMMIT" (and not additionally blocked by the plan gate) are pushed into `committedFresh`, which is the ONLY array that flows into `zerodte_setup_log` (via `commitFreshZeroDteRowsAtomic`) and therefore into grading/the public record. A WATCH (or SKIP/BLOCKED) verdict setup is never in `committedFresh`, is never upserted to `zerodte_setup_log`, and therefore can never appear in any grading pass that reads that table. This is corroborated by src/lib/zerodte/board.ts's `resolveFreshFindStatus` doc comment: 'NEVER "OPEN" (P0 fix — the one-way commit door): a fresh find has NOT been committed to the session ledger... until that row exists a find is at most WATCH — a candidate, explicitly not a position,' and by horizon-adapter.ts's explicit rule that a gate-blocked/WATCH setup is never silently promoted to COMMIT (tests in horizon-adapter.test.ts). Night Hawk's own `nighthawk_play_outcomes` table is a separate, next-day EDITION product (upsertNighthawkPlayOutcomes) whose 'pending' rows are the published Night Hawk plays, gated by an entirely separate builder (edition-builder.ts) — the question's phrasing conflates 0DTE Command's WATCH/COMMIT door with the Night Hawk edition table, but both surfaces enforce the same principle: WATCH/candidate-only rows never enter the graded population before an explicit COMMIT-equivalent write.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/zerodte/scan.ts lines ~1539-1563 (the `s.gate?.verdict === "COMMIT"` gate before `committedFresh.push(s)`); src/lib/zerodte/board.ts `resolveFreshFindStatus` docstring ("the one-way commit door"); src/lib/zerodte/horizon-adapter.ts + horizon-adapter.test.ts ("a gate-blocked setup must not be silently promoted to COMMIT").

**CQ-058** | PROVEN
A: Yes — confirmed by both the source and a dedicated prior measurement. src/lib/zerodte/cortex-gate.ts's header explicitly documents `evaluateCortexForCommit` recomputing a fresh verdict every scan pass with no memory of prior passes, and docs/audit/INTENTIONAL-DESIGN.md §2 ('Cortex veto has no hysteresis / latching (recomputed each pass)') states plainly: 'A veto is stateless: it does not latch (stay vetoed once fired) and does not dwell (require the block to persist for K passes before it bites). assessCortexVerdict folds this pass's verdict into the decision with no memory of prior passes.' This IS a deliberate design choice (the module is described as a precision layer that can only ever remove plays, and a latched veto would manufacture false negatives once the blocking condition genuinely clears). The companion audit tool `scripts/audit/veto-flicker-rate.mjs` was built specifically to measure this and its first real run (2026-08-05, 5 real sessions) found a RAW 100% flicker rate / median 1-pass-to-clear across 38/38 episodes — but investigation showed most of that was a measurement artifact (both zerodte_scan_rejections and zerodte_discovery_events are write-throttled to one row per DISTINCT state transition, not per scan pass, for sessions predating PR #1582). The one clean day with both tables live (2026-08-04) DID show real repeated flip-flopping: MSFT re-wrote a fresh veto row 15 times and INTC 6 times in one session — a real, measured instance of exactly the flicker the question describes. Verdict at the time: 'insufficient/confounded evidence — cortex-gate.ts NOT touched,' with a forward-looking re-run (post-#1582-fix sessions only) flagged as the next step.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/zerodte/cortex-gate.ts header comment; docs/audit/INTENTIONAL-DESIGN.md lines 54-77 ('MSFT re-wrote a fresh veto row 15 times and INTC 6 times across one session'); scripts/audit/veto-flicker-rate.mjs.

**CQ-059** | PROVEN
A: src/lib/platform/zerodte-sim-board.ts's own header lists exactly three independent isolation layers, all verifiable in code: (1) AUTH GATE — the ingest POST endpoint is admin-only (requireAdminApi) and the board GET route only consults the sim key when the requester is admin; (2) SEPARATE KEY — sim frames live at Redis key `zerodte:board:snapshot:sim:v1` (SIM_BOARD_SNAPSHOT_KEY), a physically distinct key from the member snapshot `zerodte:board:snapshot:v1`; the module imports only the shared cache helpers and the board payload TYPE from zerodte-service.ts, never that file's member read/write functions, so a bug here 'physically cannot corrupt what members see'; (3) OPT-IN PARAM — `shouldServeSimBoard(isAdmin, simRequested)` returns true only when BOTH `isAdmin===true` AND `simRequested===true` (`isSimRequested` only accepts the literal string '1'), and there is deliberately no third state ('non-admin + sim=1 is indistinguishable from a plain member request'). Sim writes also carry a short TTL (default 30 min, `SIM_BOARD_TTL_SEC`) so an abandoned sim self-expires. Discord: I grepped the sim module, its test file, and the admin sim-feeder script (scripts/audit/zerodte-sim-feed.mjs) for any 'discord' reference — there is NONE; the sim path has zero code path to any Discord notify function (notifyZeroDteTradeOpen etc. live in src/lib/zerodte/discord-trade-notify.ts and are never imported by zerodte-sim-board.ts or the ingest route), so sim data structurally cannot trigger a Discord trade alert. Differences from the production board path: production reads/writes the real snapshot key via zerodte-service.ts's own functions and is populated continuously by the live scan/persist pipeline; sim reads/writes only the `:sim:` key, is populated only by an admin POST to the ingest endpoint (fed by scripts/audit/zerodte-sim-feed.mjs), requires `?sim=1` to be served, and has a bounded TTL the production key does not have.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/platform/zerodte-sim-board.ts lines 1-90 (header + shouldServeSimBoard/isSimRequested/SIM_BOARD_SNAPSHOT_KEY/SIM_BOARD_TTL_SEC); no 'discord' hits in src/lib/platform/zerodte-sim-board.ts, zerodte-sim-board.test.ts, or scripts/audit/zerodte-sim-feed.mjs; docs/audit/ZERODTE-SIMULATOR.md.

**CQ-060** | PROVEN
A: NOT a shared cron. `banger-live-sync` is its own dedicated cron route (src/app/api/cron/banger-live-sync/route.ts) which the route's own header comment says is meant to piggyback on an EXISTING frequent cadence (zerodte-warm/desk-warm, ~2-5 min) rather than a new EventBridge schedule — distinct from `swing-active-refresh`, which runs its own dedicated 15-minute RTH cadence (src/lib/swing/active-refresh-claim.ts: 'active-refresh uses one global "running" key because there is only one refresh cadence (15m RTH)'). Yes — a banger OPEN position and a swing OPEN position can exist simultaneously for the same ticker with conflicting direction, at the DATABASE level: banger_positions and swing_positions are fully separate tables with fully separate discovery/commit pipelines (src/lib/banger/commit.ts, src/lib/swing/commit.ts), and I grepped both commit modules for any cross-desk awareness ('swing_positions', 'swingPositions', 'cross.desk', 'other desk') — zero hits in either direction; neither desk's commit path ever checks the other desk's open positions before opening. Banger is always LONG-direction by construction (calls-only breakout engine — horizonPlayFromBangerPosition in src/lib/swing/banger-lane-merge.ts hardcodes `direction: "LONG"`), so the only real conflict scenario is banger LONG vs a swing SHORT on the same name — and nothing in either commit path prevents that. The ONLY precedence rule that exists is a DISPLAY-layer dedup, not a commit-time rule: `mergeBangerPositionsIntoSwingPlays` (banger-lane-merge.ts lines 176-197) builds a `managingTickers` set of every ticker already MANAGING as a swing play, and filters banger rows to exclude any ticker already in that set (`bangerToAdd = bangerPlays.filter(p => !managingTickers.has(...))`) — so on the Command Deck a member never sees both a swing and a banger card for the same ticker (swing wins the display slot), but this says nothing about whether both desks are simultaneously risking real capital in opposite directions on that name underneath the hood.
EVIDENCE: src/app/api/cron/banger-live-sync/route.ts header comment; src/lib/swing/active-refresh-claim.ts header ('active-refresh uses one global running key because there is only one refresh cadence (15m RTH)'); src/lib/swing/banger-lane-merge.ts lines 39-70 (`direction: "LONG"` hardcoded) and lines 176-197 (`mergeBangerPositionsIntoSwingPlays` display dedup); zero grep hits for cross-desk checks in src/lib/banger/commit.ts or src/lib/swing/commit.ts.

**CQ-061** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: PRs #3903 (swing, commit 17c08b015) and #3911 (banger, commit c26d8975c) both exist and both wire alerts through the shared 'Chief Trade Bot' HTTP integration (src/lib/zerodte/discord-trade-notify.ts's postChiefTrade, reused by src/lib/swing/discord-trade-notify.ts and src/lib/banger/discord-trade-notify.ts). Gating found: (1) a single global env-var kill switch per desk — `swingDiscordAlertsEnabled()`/`zerodteDiscordAlertsEnabled()` check `SWING_DISCORD_ALERTS`/`ZERODTE_DISCORD_ALERTS` ("1"/"true"/"yes") — and (2) a fixed channel ID per desk (`SWING_CHIEF_TRADE_CHANNEL_ID`). There is NO per-member opt-in and NO per-member tier check anywhere in swing/discord-trade-notify.ts, banger/discord-trade-notify.ts, or zerodte/discord-trade-notify.ts — I grepped all three files for tier/opt-in/subscribe logic and found none; this is a broadcast alert into ONE shared Discord channel via a bot (members presumably self-select by joining that channel/server), not an individualized per-member notification, so 'member opt-in' as a code-level gate does not exist — it is entirely external to this codebase (Discord server membership), which is a genuine gap the question correctly flags if individualized consent was ever promised. On the 429/retry question: postChiefTrade (discord-trade-notify.ts lines 125-158) makes exactly ONE fetch attempt; on any non-2xx response including 429 it does `console.warn(...); return false;` — there is NO retry, NO backoff, and NO queue. A Discord API 429 during a market-open alert burst means that specific trade-open/close alert is silently dropped (logged only to console) with no re-delivery mechanism.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/swing/discord-trade-notify.ts (swingDiscordAlertsEnabled/swingChiefTradeChannelId, no tier/opt-in check); src/lib/zerodte/discord-trade-notify.ts lines 125-158 (postChiefTrade, single fetch, no retry, `if (!res.ok) { console.warn(...); return false; }`); commits 17c08b015 and c26d8975c commit messages confirm env-gated wiring 'mirrors the 0DTE/Legacy notification pattern.' A live check to confirm real-world behavior: force a 429 from a test Discord webhook proxy during a simulated commit burst and confirm the alert is dropped with only a console log (not retried) — not run here since it requires a live/mocked Discord endpoint.

**CQ-063** | PROVEN
A: src/lib/swing/active-refresh-claim.ts defines the exact bound: `SWING_ACTIVE_REFRESH_MAX_MS = 180_000` (matches the route's own maxDuration=180s) plus `SWING_ACTIVE_REFRESH_CLAIM_BUFFER_MS = 15_000`, and `activeRefreshClaimTtlSec()` returns `Math.ceil((180_000 + 15_000)/1000)` = 195 seconds. The claim is acquired via `sharedCacheSetNx(SWING_ACTIVE_REFRESH_CLAIM_KEY, ..., activeRefreshClaimTtlSec(), ...)` in src/app/api/cron/swing-active-refresh/route.ts (Redis SET NX EX, same pattern the standing performance mandate names for sharedCacheSetNx). Since the claim is a Redis key with a hard 195-second EXPIRE baked in at creation, if the claim holder dies mid-refresh (crash, OOM, timeout) the key self-expires 195 seconds after acquisition regardless of process state — no separate heartbeat/liveness mechanism is needed, and no replica can be permanently blocked. On a clean exit the route also explicitly deletes the key (`sharedCacheDel(SWING_ACTIVE_REFRESH_CLAIM_KEY)` at route.ts line ~471), so the normal case releases immediately; only a hard-crash case waits out the full 195s TTL. Given the refresh cadence is 15 minutes (900s), a worst-case 195s stuck-claim delay is well within one cadence window and does not risk skipping a whole cycle.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/swing/active-refresh-claim.ts lines 1-33 (SWING_ACTIVE_REFRESH_MAX_MS=180_000, SWING_ACTIVE_REFRESH_CLAIM_BUFFER_MS=15_000, activeRefreshClaimTtlSec()); src/app/api/cron/swing-active-refresh/route.ts lines 174-177 (sharedCacheSetNx call) and ~471 (sharedCacheDel on completion); commit 95238a727 (#3899) title 'fix(swing): Q37 singleton active-refresh claim + roll revalidation'.

**CQ-064** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: PR #3909 (commit 7ee7e1318, dated 2026-09-05 — today) added src/lib/swing/ex-dividend-adjustment.ts + ex-dividend-reads.ts, wired into src/lib/swing/manage.ts (`underlyingPriceForStructuralStop`, imported and applied at manage.ts line ~179 before the structural_stop compare). On an ex-div session for a LONG position it adds the cash dividend back to the underlying spot BEFORE the structural_stop comparison ('LONG: add cash dividend back (mechanical open gap). SHORT: no adjustment needed'), specifically to prevent a mechanical ex-div opening gap from false-triggering a capital-preservation EXIT. Critically: the adjustment applies ONLY to the comparison value used to decide whether EXIT fires — it does NOT change the stop LEVEL itself that a member sees (`structuralStopLevel: numOrNull(reads.structuralStopLevel ?? row.thesis_invalidation_px)` in manage-sync.ts line 306 is unadjusted, still reading straight off `thesis_invalidation_px`). Furthermore, I grepped src/lib/swing/discord-trade-notify.ts for any 'stop' field in the alert payload and found none — the BTO/STC ChiefTradePayload only ever carries ticker/strike/expiry/price/qty, never a stop price. So the premise of the question (was the Discord alert updated with a new stop price) does not apply: there is no stop-price field in ANY swing Discord alert, ex-div or otherwise, and the stop LEVEL a member would see anywhere in the UI is never adjusted by this fix (only the internal EXIT-trigger comparison is) — so nothing needs updating in the alert, but this also means the fix is invisible to the member: they never see 'why' a stop held through an ex-div gap because the displayed stop number never moves. Since this PR is dated the same day as 'today,' there is no historical real swing example that has already gone through an ex-div session under this code to point to; the harness's own regression tests (ex-dividend-adjustment.test.ts) use synthetic fixtures only ($98 spot + $2 dividend = $100 adjusted compare for LONG). A live check that would prove this in production: find a swing LONG position open on a real ex-dividend date via /api/market/swing/record or the admin discovery-debug route, confirm `exDividendSession`/`exDividendCash` populate in that day's snapshot, and confirm the position did NOT exit on structural_stop despite the mechanical gap.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/swing/ex-dividend-adjustment.ts (underlyingPriceForStructuralStop, LONG-only +cash adjustment); src/lib/swing/manage.ts line ~179 (`underlyingPriceForStructuralStop` call before structural_stop compare); src/lib/swing/manage-sync.ts line 306 (structuralStopLevel reads unadjusted `row.thesis_invalidation_px`); src/lib/swing/discord-trade-notify.ts (ChiefTradePayload shape has no stop field); commit 7ee7e1318 dated 2026-09-05 (today), no prior real-world ex-div swing example exists yet.

One day later, PR #2893 (commit 5ee65981b, 'fix(0dte): restore unified G-3 score floor 65 — stop death-band BREAKOUT commits') reverted the floor back to 65 in production, citing REAL live RTH evidence from 2026-08-25: the lowered floor produced 5 real OPEN commits in the 50-64 'death band' (MSTR 54, LUNR 53, ASST 59), and the engine's own calibration data (F-2) measured that 55-64 score band at 18.8% win rate / -24.5% avg P&L — below breakeven. Current src/lib/zerodte/gates.ts (HEAD) confirms `ZERODTE_SCORE_FLOOR_BREAKOUT = envInt("ZERODTE_SCORE_FLOOR_BREAKOUT", 65)` and `ZERODTE_SCORE_FLOOR_PIN = envInt("ZERODTE_SCORE_FLOOR_PIN", 65)` — i.e. the ORIGINAL 65 floor is what's live today, not #2846's 50. So: WR before/after for the #2846 change specifically was never properly measured before shipping (an unmeasured optimization, exactly the failure mode the question worries about), and the floor is NOT active in production — it was walked back to 65 within 24 hours based on real losing-trade evidence.
EVIDENCE: commit 0d5086a51 (#2846) message — floor 65->50 justified only by publish-count/rejection-rate, no WR backtest for the floor itself; commit 5ee65981b (#2893) message — 'Engine calibration (F-2) measured 55-64 at 18.8% WR / -24.5% avg — below breakeven'; current src/lib/zerodte/gates.ts lines 307-308 show `envInt("ZERODTE_SCORE_FLOOR_BREAKOUT", 65)` / `envInt("ZERODTE_SCORE_FLOOR_PIN", 65)`; `git log --oneline -S'ZERODTE_SCORE_FLOOR_BREAKOUT = envInt("ZERODTE_SCORE_FLOOR_BREAKOUT", 65)' -- src/lib/zerodte/gates.ts` shows the 65->50->65 flip sequence across 0d5086a51 and 5ee65981b.

**CQ-066** | PROVEN
A: src/app/api/market/nighthawk/edition/route.ts's `resolveNighthawkEdition` function has an explicit, tested fallback chain for exactly this case. If the exact `editionFor` date has no row (e.g. Monday's 6am cron never fired), it falls through to `fetchLatestNighthawkEdition()` — the most recent PUBLISHED edition (e.g. the prior Friday's). That edition is served with an honest staleness marker: `if (edition.edition_for && edition.edition_for !== editionFor) { edition.stale = true; edition.served_for = edition.edition_for; }` — so members get a real, stale-flagged edition with a banner, NOT a blank/empty state, PROVIDED the last real edition is recent enough: a `MAX_EDITION_AGE_DAYS = 4` guard fails CLOSED — if the latest available edition is more than 4 calendar days older than the requested `editionFor` (or the age computation is non-finite, e.g. from a corrupted date), the function instead returns `emptyEdition(editionFor)`, whose `recap_summary` reads 'Tonight's edition publishes after the close. Five ranked plays land here automatically.' — the genuine empty/pending state, `available:false`. So the answer is: BOTH behaviors exist and are chosen based on staleness age — a single missed Monday run (1 day stale, well under the 4-day cap) is served as the last edition with `stale:true`/`served_for` set (stale banner), never an empty state; only a much longer outage (>4 days with no publish) degrades to the empty 'publishes after the close' state. A legacy engine-proxy fallback (`fetchLegacyPlays()`) also exists as a last resort before the empty state if `BLACKOUT_INTEL_URL` is configured.
EVIDENCE: src/app/api/market/nighthawk/edition/route.ts lines 191-241 (`resolveNighthawkEdition`): `const latest = await fetchLatestNighthawkEdition(); ... const MAX_EDITION_AGE_DAYS = 4; ... if (!Number.isFinite(edAge) || edAge > MAX_EDITION_AGE_DAYS) { return emptyEdition(editionFor); } if (edition.edition_for !== editionFor) { edition.stale = true; edition.served_for = edition.edition_for; }`; `emptyEdition()` function (lines ~80-90) with its 'publishes after the close' copy.

For BOTH bmo and amc (`spansClosedMarket`, line 286), reaction_pct is computed as `pctChange(priorClose, anchorBar.c)` (line 292) where priorCloseYmd for BMO is `prevSessionYmd(orderedYmds, reportYmd)` (the close BEFORE the report date) and for AMC is `reportYmd` itself (the close on the print day, before the after-hours print). The code's own extensive comment block (lines 67-143) documents this was WRONG before 2026-08-22 for BMO (open→close read, inverting sign on 27% of prints, e.g. DDOG 2026-08-06 gapped -19.68% but an open->close read would have shown a small gain) and cites a 206-print AMC measurement (mean |overnight gap| 7.41% vs mean |open->close| 3.05%) and a 519-print BMO measurement (27.0% sign-flip rate) as the evidence base for the current anchoring. I could not run a live API call against a real current AMC ticker in this pass (no production credentials minted in this session); the unit test file meridian-reaction-core.test.ts exercises this logic with fixture bars, which I did not re-run here.

**CQ-070** | PARTIALLY PROVEN

The function that USED to merge Benzinga against a separate 'grid'/UW calendar, `mergeEarningsTimelineSources` (meridian-benzinga-earnings-core.ts:205-247), is explicitly marked `@deprecated` ('UW grid removed') and its only caller, `mergeBenzingaTimelineRows` (line 250), has ZERO callers anywhere else in the codebase outside tests (`grep` confirms) — this is dead code. For the historical record of 'which wins': in that deprecated function, when a grid-sourced row's ticker/date key doesn't match an existing Benzinga key, and Benzinga separately has a row for that ticker at a DIFFERENT date with `date_status === 'confirmed'`, the grid row is DROPPED (line 235-237: `if (bz && bz.date !== date && bz.date_status === 'confirmed') { continue; }`) — i.e. Benzinga's confirmed date always won over the grid/UW date. That policy is preserved in comments but no longer executed in production. I did not find any other place in the repo where a second, independent earnings-date source is reconciled against Benzinga for Meridian.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/meridian/meridian-timeline-server.ts:141,176 (current live pipeline, single Benzinga source); src/lib/meridian/meridian-benzinga-earnings-core.ts:201-247 (@deprecated mergeEarningsTimelineSources, conflict-resolution logic: confirmed Benzinga date wins), :249-259 (mergeBenzingaTimelineRows, its only caller, zero non-test callers found via grep).

## H. Largo

**CQ-075** | PROVEN
A: Yes — verifyClaims returns coverage:null on total===0, and this is NOT a safety net against fabrication on an extraction miss: it only means 'no numeric claims found in the text'. If the model's prose contains zero numbers matching the regex in extractNumericClaims (src/lib/bie/verifier.ts:44-65) — e.g. it states a qualitative/directional claim like 'NVDA flow looks bullish going into the print' with no digits, or a subtle claim that fails the regex (a spelled-out number, a ticker-adjacent number the regex mis-tokenizes) — verifyClaims short-circuits to {total:0, coverage:null} at line 150, and applyVerificationCaveat (turn-outcome.ts:36-46) requires total>=4 AND coverage<0.5 to append the 'Grounding note' caveat. total===0 never meets total>=4, so NO caveat is ever appended, and the answer ships with zero disclosure even though it may contain unverifiable qualitative claims. unverifiedTurn() (bie/verifier.ts:38-40) is the constructor used ONLY when there is literally no answer to verify (error paths) — it is not itself a vector for fabrication, but it documents the same design principle (never invent coverage:1) that CLAIM extraction can silently fail to trigger. A stress case: ask about a ticker with thin/empty tool results and a system prompt that still permits directional language (see CQ-085) — if the model writes 2-3 numbers or none, verifyClaims's LARGO_GROUNDING_MIN_CLAIMS=4 threshold (verifier.ts:124) means the caveat never fires regardless of whether those numbers are real. This is a genuine gap: the verifier is a claims-count-gated numeric check, not an extraction-miss detector.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/bie/verifier.ts:38-40 (unverifiedTurn), :44-65 (extractNumericClaims), :146-174 (verifyClaims, coverage:null at total===0); src/lib/largo/turn-outcome.ts:36-46 (applyVerificationCaveat gate total>=4 && coverage<0.5); LARGO_GROUNDING_MIN_CLAIMS=4 at verifier.ts:124.

**CQ-077** | PROVEN
A: Yes, confirmed by code — the 'cache readers only' rule is NOT universal in run-tool.ts. At least 7 tool cases call live Polygon endpoints with NO cache wrapper at all, on every single Largo invocation: get_oi_per_strike (run-tool.ts:397), get_oi_per_expiry (:416), get_max_pain (:433), get_greeks (:447), get_atm_chains (:469), get_options_chain (:487), get_options_volume (:507) — all of these route through polygonChainBundle() (run-tool.ts:253-256) → fetchPolygonOptionsChain() (polygon-options-gex.ts:4314-4326) → fetchChainBand() (polygon-options-gex.ts:4150-4204+), and fetchChainBand makes a direct `polygonFetchUrl` snapshot-options call with NO serverCache/TTL wrapper anywhere in its body (confirmed by grep — the file's only two serverCache usages, at lines 1949 and 4235, are for dividend-yield and front-gamma-coefficient lookups, not the chain fetch itself). So every one of those 7 Largo tools hits live Polygon per Largo-turn, uncached. By contrast, UW reads in the same file DO go through a rate-limited, cache-wrapped path (uw-rate-limiter.ts/uw-shared-cache.ts, with runUwPooled and a documented 'cache-reader-rule' comment in uw-hunt-budget.ts:14-24), and the term 'runWithBackgroundUwSweep' the question names does not appear in run-tool.ts at all — it only exists in CRON warming routes (darkpool-discord, swing-discovery, desk-warm, etc.) that pre-warm shared caches for those crons, separate from Largo's tool dispatch. So the literal answer: run-tool.ts's Polygon options-chain family of tools is a real, code-confirmed violation of the stated cache-reader rule; the UW side is materially closer to compliant (rate-limited + cached) but still per-request rather than exclusively cache-warmed.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/largo/run-tool.ts:253-256 (polygonChainBundle), :397,416,433,447,469,487,507 (tool cases calling it); src/lib/providers/polygon-options-gex.ts:4314-4326 (fetchPolygonOptionsChain), :4150-4204 (fetchChainBand, direct polygonFetchUrl call, no serverCache wrapper); src/lib/providers/uw-hunt-budget.ts:14-24 ('cache-reader-rule enforcement' doc, confirms the rule is a real named policy elsewhere in the codebase, applied to UW but bypassed for this Polygon chain path).

**CQ-080** | PROVEN
A: Referent resolution is deterministic code, NOT a model guess and NOT 'last tool result' — it is a last-known-ticker-in-recent-user-turns regex scan. extractTicker(question, historyText) in question-intent.ts:212-248 first scans the CURRENT question text for an uppercase 2-5 letter symbol (last match wins, question-intent.ts:215-238); if none is found there, it falls back to scanning `recentUserText(history, 6)` (the last 6 messages, question-intent.ts:110-114) concatenated with the current question, again taking the LAST matched KNOWN_TICKERS symbol (question-intent.ts:239-246) — so 'what about it?' after discussing SPX then AAPL resolves to AAPL (the more recently mentioned ticker), by plain regex/string matching, not an LLM call. One caveat worth flagging: despite its name, recentUserText does NOT actually filter by message role — it maps `history.slice(-6)` regardless of role (user or assistant) and only skips non-string content, so a plain-string assistant message containing an uppercase ticker mention could also feed the fallback scan; this is a minor naming/behavior mismatch, not tested against directly. Session state itself (ConversationMemoryState in conversation-memory.ts:27-56) also holds a `ticker` field with a documented 5-min-or-ticker-change expiry policy (module doc lines 17-22), used to reuse cached consensus rather than re-deriving referent from scratch — but the primary pronoun resolution mechanism that decides WHICH ticker 'it' means is extractTicker, not this memory struct.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/largo/question-intent.ts:212-248 (extractTicker), :110-114 (recentUserText, no role filter despite name); src/lib/largo/conversation-memory.ts:9-22 (module doc: lifecycle/expiry), :27-56 (ConversationMemoryState schema).

**CQ-081** | DISPROVEN
A: The premise conflates two separate mechanisms. applyVerificationCaveat (turn-outcome.ts:36-46) has NOTHING to do with an asOf/staleness age — it is a NUMERIC-CLAIM-COVERAGE caveat: it fires when total>=4 numeric claims AND coverage<0.5 (i.e. fewer than half the numbers in the answer could be matched against context data), producing 'Grounding note. N of M figures... could not be traced to data pulled this turn.' There is no per-tool suppression list — it is called unconditionally at both call sites in largo-terminal.ts (lines 1090, 1397) with no tool-name branching. Separately, actual 'is this data stale' freshness DOES exist but lives in a completely different module — src/lib/bie/vector-state-freshness.ts, which classifies Vector desk-state age via freshnessFromAgeMs with boundaries live<60s, recent<10min, stale beyond 10min (vector-state-freshness.ts:47-49) — NOT a flat 5-minute threshold, and NOT wired into applyVerificationCaveat at all; it's a distinct field (observed_at/age_seconds/freshness) returned inside get_vector_full_state/get_vector_pulse tool payloads for the model to read and optionally mention, with no automatic caveat-append mechanism gating on it. So: no, applyVerificationCaveat does not append a stale-data warning at any asOf threshold — that check doesn't exist in this function; the actual staleness signal (10-min 'stale' boundary, not 5 min) is a separate, non-enforcing data field.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/largo/turn-outcome.ts:36-46 (applyVerificationCaveat, coverage-only, no asOf logic); src/lib/bie/vector-state-freshness.ts:47-49 (VECTOR_FRESHNESS_LIVE_SEC=60, VECTOR_FRESHNESS_RECENT_SEC=600 i.e. 10 min, not 5); src/lib/largo-terminal.ts:1090,1397 (unconditional calls, no per-tool suppression).

**CQ-083** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: Mixed, not uniformly one global timestamp — and the mix itself is a real defect surface. getPlatformSnapshot (platform/index.ts:28-89) stamps ONE top-level `as_of = new Date().toISOString()` at line 34, computed BEFORE any of the parallel sub-fetches (spx/flows/nighthawk/largo) even start, and independent of whether any of them return null on failure or serve stale cached data. Whether that hides per-desk staleness depends on the sub-shape: SpxDeskSummary DOES carry its own `as_of: string` field (platform/types.ts:9-10) — so a stale SPX read is in principle detectable by comparing snapshot.as_of vs snapshot.spx.as_of. But FlowTapeSummary (types.ts:61-85) carries NO timestamp/freshness field of its own at all — if the flow tape summary is served from a stale cache, nothing in the composed PlatformSnapshot reveals it; only the outer as_of (which reflects the wrapper's own compute time, not the flow data's age) is visible. NightHawkEditionSummary carries `published_at` (types.ts:90), which is the edition's publish time, a reasonable proxy but not literally 'when this read happened'. So: yes, a single global timestamp CAN hide partial staleness — specifically for the flows sub-object, which has no per-desk freshness field to fall back on.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/platform/index.ts:28-89 (getPlatformSnapshot, single as_of at line 34); src/lib/platform/types.ts:9-10 (SpxDeskSummary.as_of present), :61-85 (FlowTapeSummary — no as_of/freshness field), :87-94 (NightHawkEditionSummary.published_at), :96-99 (PlatformSnapshot.as_of top-level).

**CQ-084** | UNKNOWN
A: Cannot verify from static code alone — verifyClaims (bie/verifier.ts:146-174) can only check whether a claimed NUMBER matches something present in the tool/feed context data (spot=5500, gamma_flip=5450 would both be present as raw context numbers); it has no arithmetic-relationship checker. If the model states '60 points' when the correct distance is 50, verifyClaims's matches() function (verifier.ts:153-160) checks the claimed number 60 against candidates derived from EACH context number individually (src, src*2, src*0.5, -src, src*100, src/100) — it does NOT compute derived quantities like differences between two context numbers (5500-5450=50). So a wrong arithmetic answer of '60 points' would need 60 (or a scaled/negated variant of some single context number) to coincidentally match a REAL context value to be falsely verified, and would otherwise land in `unverified` — but even when in `unverified`, that only triggers a caveat if total claims >=4 AND coverage<0.5 (see CQ-081), so a SINGLE wrong arithmetic claim buried among several correct ones would very likely NOT trigger the caveat at all (coverage would stay >=0.5). So the verifier is not designed to catch subtraction/arithmetic errors specifically — it is a presence check on raw context numbers, not a computed-relationship check. Live reproduction would be needed to confirm this in practice.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/bie/verifier.ts:146-174 (verifyClaims — no derived-difference computation, only src/2×/0.5×/negation/×100/÷100 candidates per single context number). LIVE CHECK: send 'SPX is at 5500, gamma flip at 5450, how far below flip?' to a live Largo turn forcing the model to answer '60 points' via a crafted feed, and inspect the verification object in the response diagnostics.

**CQ-085** | PARTIALLY PROVEN

Do not force a clean story' (system-prompt.ts:240) and 'Sparse flow — if tape is thin, say flow light... do not fill gaps with narrative' (system-prompt.ts:239) — both are narrow (contradictions / thin-flow specifically), not a general mandatory template for the case 'all tools return neutral/empty'. There is no verbatim instruction like 'when all signals are neutral you MUST say no edge exists and MUST NOT use directional language.' Verified against the enforcement side too: verifyClaims/applyVerificationCaveat (see CQ-075/081) only gate on NUMERIC claim coverage, not on qualitative directional language — a response like 'NVDA looks primed for a move higher' contains zero numbers and would sail through verifyClaims with total:0/coverage:null, triggering no caveat regardless of whether any tool result supported that direction. Temperature is set via `loopTemperature = params.temperature ?? TEMPERATURE` (anthropic.ts, generic default) with nothing tool-conditioned to zero it out on neutral results. So the P0 risk named in the exercise brief is real and not closed by code: a genuinely neutral/empty tool-result set can still produce directional prose, and nothing downstream of the model catches it.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/largo/system-prompt.ts:239-240 (only relevant guidance, narrow scope, no mandatory neutral-template text); src/lib/bie/verifier.ts (verifyClaims checks numbers only, not qualitative/directional language — see CQ-075/084 evidence). LIVE CHECK: send a question about a genuinely flat/neutral ticker with an empty feed and inspect whether the answer contains directional words (bullish/bearish/likely to move) absent any supporting tool number.

**CQ-086** | PROVEN
A: Free-text, not machine-verified against tool output IDs — and the gap is real and exploitable. The Largo Claude-tool-loop path (the live production path per turn-outcome.ts's CLAUDE_TURN_BUCKET) has no citation-to-source-ID binding at all; the 'citation' machinery that DOES exist in the codebase (cortex-read.ts:671-722 citationFromView, nighthawk-edition-read.ts:1198-1263) belongs to the older deterministic BIE composer path, not the current Claude tool loop that answers real member questions. The only verification applied to a Claude-loop answer is verifyClaims (bie/verifier.ts), and it checks NUMBER-PRESENCE only — matches() (verifier.ts:153-160) tests whether a claimed number appears ANYWHERE among ALL context numbers (flattened via collectContextNumbers, which walks every tool result and the live feed indiscriminately, verifier.ts:69-88), with NO requirement that the number is bound to the LABEL the model attaches to it. So yes: the model can write 'Thermal gamma flip 7450' where 7450 is a real number pulled this turn (e.g. it's actually the MAX PAIN level from a different tool, or SPX's own spot price scaled), and verifyClaims would mark it verified=true because 7450 exists somewhere in context — the check has no notion of which tool/field a number came from, only that some number equal to (or a scaled/negated variant of) it exists. This is a stronger, worse defect than CQ-084's arithmetic gap: a wrong LABEL on a real number reads as fully verified.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/bie/verifier.ts:69-88 (collectContextNumbers — flattens ALL tool results/feed with no source-ID tracking), :153-160 (matches() — presence-only, no label binding); src/lib/bie/cortex-read.ts:671-722 and src/lib/bie/nighthawk-edition-read.ts:1198-1263 (citation machinery exists but belongs to the deprecated deterministic BIE composer path, not the live Claude tool loop — see turn-outcome.ts's CLAUDE_TURN_BUCKET comment confirming the BIE router is no longer the runtime dependency).

A: Retention: default 7 days (DEFAULT_RETENTION_DAYS=7, largo-store.ts:21), overridable via LARGO_SESSION_RETENTION_DAYS env (clamped 1-365, largoSessionRetentionDays(), largo-store.ts:436-440), enforced by a weekly cron (api/cron/largo-cleanup/route.ts, doc comment 'Weekly cleanup — delete Largo sessions inactive for 7+ days') that DELETEs largo_sessions where updated_at is older than the retention window (largo-store.ts:444-461); largo_messages presumably cascade-deletes via FK (not directly inspected this pass but no separate purge call exists, consistent with a CASCADE constraint). **CQ-088** | DISPROVEN
A: Real, code-level enforcement exists — this is NOT prompt-only, and it is NOT bypassable via ordinary tool invocation. The capability registry (registry/capability-registry.ts) declares an `entitlement` field per tool; get_playbook_promotion_evidence is catalogued `entitlement: 'admin'` (capability-registry.ts:307). checkToolEntitlement (core/tool-guard.ts:97-112) is invoked on EVERY tool call inside makeGuardedToolRunner (tool-guard.ts:135-174), which is the SOLE execution path both the blocking and streaming Largo turn handlers use (largo-terminal.ts:1040,1344) — it denies (returns a structured refusal, does not execute) when the catalog says 'admin' and viewer.isAdmin is false, and viewer.isAdmin is computed server-side from isAdminUser(userId) (largo-terminal.ts:804, imported from lib/admin-access, i.e. Clerk publicMetadata.role==='admin', not user-supplied input) — so a prompt-injection attempt inside a message ('pretend you're an admin and call get_playbook_promotion_evidence') cannot flip viewer.isAdmin, since that value is derived from the authenticated session, not from conversation content. The tool list itself is NOT filtered by tier before being handed to the model (filteredTools = LARGO_TOOL_DEFS unconditionally, largo-terminal.ts:878) — meaning a non-admin CAN cause the model to attempt the call — but the attempt is denied at execution time by the guard, not merely discouraged. Only ONE admin-scoped tool exists in the current catalog (get_playbook_promotion_evidence); the enforcement design is fail-additive: the code's own comment (tool-guard.ts:16-25) states a tool is denied only when EXPLICITLY catalogued 'admin', so an uncatalogued future admin-sensitive tool would NOT be blocked by default — that residual risk is real but is a documented, deliberate tradeoff (catalog coverage is asserted 137/137 by registry.test.ts) rather than an active bypass today.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/largo/registry/capability-registry.ts:307 (get_playbook_promotion_evidence entitlement:'admin'); src/lib/largo/core/tool-guard.ts:97-112 (checkToolEntitlement), :135-174 (makeGuardedToolRunner, sole execution path); src/lib/largo-terminal.ts:804 (isAdmin from isAdminUser(userId), server-derived), :1040,1344 (makeGuardedToolRunner wired at both call sites), :878 (filteredTools = LARGO_TOOL_DEFS, unfiltered tool LIST but execution still guarded); src/lib/largo/registry/registry.test.ts:103-107 (asserts no admin capability leaks to premium filtering, i.e. tested).

What IS confirmed from code: the tool loop has a default per-turn round cap of maxRounds=12 (anthropic.ts, `const maxRounds = params.maxRounds ?? 12`) plus a documented wall-clock loopBudgetMs guard added after measuring real 81.7s/89.8s/98.3s prod turns on 2026-08-20 (comment on the loopBudgetMs param, anthropic.ts). LIVE CHECK: CloudWatch Logs Insights on /ecs/blackout-production filtered for the emptyAnswerFallback/max_rounds stop-reason string over a 7-day window, plus a direct query against whatever table logBieInteraction (turn-outcome.ts:48-61) writes to for token/latency fields, or the Anthropic Console usage API for the org's actual per-day input/output token totals.

## I. Cross-Product Correctness

**CQ-090** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: Canonical gamma flip IS unified at the data layer, and cross-product divergence has been measured, documented, and fixed at least twice — but no live 4-way capture exists to state a max-divergence number for the last RTH day. Code evidence: `src/lib/providers/polygon-options-gex.ts`'s `fetchGexHeatmap()` is 'the ONE source every other tool/service/AI surface consumes' (FINDINGS.md:3431), and SPX Slayer, Thermal, Vector's default wall/flip calc all trace to the same `wallsFromStrikeTotals`/`computeGexWalls` lineage over that one matrix (FINDINGS.md:4466-4474). `gamma-desk.ts:58-68` documents a 2026-07-21 unification of `computeGammaFlip` specifically to remove 'the cross-surface disagreement and the below-spot inversion documented in docs/audit/FINDINGS.md.' HOWEVER, FINDINGS.md:4456-4500 ('Several Largo-facing Vector reads silently defaulted to 0DTE-scoped walls/flip instead of the canonical all-expiry picture — FIXED') is a concrete, previously-real divergence: six Largo call sites (largo/status route, slash-prompts.ts, mini-panel.ts, desk-scope-prefetch.ts) omitted the `horizon` param on `fetchVectorFullState`, silently getting Vector's 0DTE-scoped BSM-reconstructed flip/walls (`vector-dte-walls-core.ts`, a genuinely different calculation/data source) instead of the canonical all-expiry matrix SPX Slayer/Thermal use by default — a real, live cross-product flip mismatch that shipped to members via Largo until fixed. Separately, `vector-gex-reconstruct.ts:255-300` (`gammaFlipFromLadder`) is a THIRD, client-side independent flip reconstruction with its own comments documenting historical live divergence ('SPX weekly banner showed flip 5,996 with spot 7,522 (−20%) while the API ... showed 7,995'). No committed tolerance-doc or automated 4-way comparator was found (only `desk-verifier.ts`/`heatmap-verifier.ts`, which check `.flip` against the shared `cumulativeGammaFlip`, not a cross-product diff).
EVIDENCE: src/lib/providers/gamma-desk.ts:57-68; docs/audit/FINDINGS.md:3431 (import-graph proof); docs/audit/FINDINGS.md:4456-4500 (Largo horizon-omission bug, FIXED); src/features/vector/lib/vector-gex-reconstruct.ts:255-300 (documented live divergence, e.g. 'weekly flip 5,991 and 0DTE flip 5,400, ~20-30% below spot' at spot 7,575).

**CQ-091** | PROVEN
A: Yes — this is explicitly surfaced as intentional disagreement, never silently shown to members without framing. `src/lib/largo/system-prompt.ts:320-345` ('Cross-product questions — one tool, and NEVER resolve a split (non-negotiable)') names the exact example in the question verbatim ('where do Helix and Vector disagree') as a case Largo must route through `get_cross_product_read`, which returns a `split` verdict the model is instructed to report as-is: 'Report BOTH readings with their evidence and stop there. Do not resolve it, do not pick a side ... The disagreement IS the finding.' The general 'Conflicts' contract at lines 51-55, 240, 263, 328, 506-513 requires Largo to name every place 'flow against structure, one desk against another' disagrees, and explicitly forbids smoothing it into a clean story. So a Helix-bullish/Night-Hawk-bearish conflict is architecturally designed to surface as a labeled disagreement in any Largo cross-product answer, not silently shown. This does not address the raw UI (Helix panel + Night Hawk panel with no visual cross-link) — a member scanning the two desks directly (not through Largo) sees no automatic cross-signal callout; only Largo's synthesis layer carries this contract.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/largo/system-prompt.ts:51-55, 320-345 (verbatim 'where do Helix and Vector disagree' example + non-negotiable split-reporting rule).

**CQ-092** | PARTIALLY PROVEN

What DOES exist is the Largo synthesis-layer rule (system-prompt.ts:320-345) that treats any such disagreement as a 'split' to report, not reconcile — i.e., the platform's answer to 'is this allowed' is 'yes, by design, and it must be surfaced, not silently smoothed,' rather than a hard product rule preventing the two boards from disagreeing. `docs/audit/INTENTIONAL-DESIGN.md` item (1), the FLOW-first merge precedence, and Cortex's `gex-walls` oppose weight (cortex-oppose-magnitude-ab.mjs) are the closest thing to a cross-signal gate, but those operate WITHIN the 0DTE engine's own commit pipeline (Cortex vetoes/opposes a 0DTE play using GEX-wall evidence), not against SPX Slayer's separate play output. No file was found that reads SPX Slayer's current play direction as an input to 0DTE gating or vice versa.

So 'Vector ranks Y as top gamma-shift leader, Thermal shows Y flat' is explained: Vector's 0DTE-scoped ranking can diverge from Thermal's all-expiry read on the SAME ticker by design — not necessarily a bug, UNLESS Vector's ranking is being read through a Largo/UI surface that omits the horizon and silently inherits 0DTE scope where 'all' was intended, which is the exact bug six call sites had (now fixed per that same FINDINGS entry). So at minimum THREE different freshness-field shapes exist across desks (`as_of` UTC build time; `polled_at`/`as_of` desk-instant; per-item quote timestamp with no top-level `as_of`), and no shared normalizer to reconcile them for a member/Largo comparing desk freshness was found in this pass.

**CQ-096** | DISPROVEN
A: The premise that precision SHOULD be identical is false by explicit design, and the code documents this as intentional, not a defect. `src/lib/track-record-public.ts:29-42` ('formatPercent') states: 'The internal /track-record page (dp=1) and this public projection (dp=0) both route through this so the two displayed numbers are always the same fraction rounded by the same rule at different precision, never two independently-hand-written formulas that can double-round-diverge.' The public route (`pct(x)` = `formatPercent(x,0)`, integer) and the internal desk `/api/market/zerodte/record` (`round1()`, 1 decimal, e.g. `record.ts:565` and confirmed by `record.test.ts:669`: 'win rate is wins/graded ... 33.3') deliberately differ in decimal places. This is SAFE precisely because both derive from the SAME underlying `wins/n` fraction through a single shared formatter (`formatPercent`) rather than two independently computed pipelines — so there is no 'trust violation' double-rounding drift of the kind #3751/roundFloats was built to prevent; the two numbers are consistent projections of one number at two precisions, not two competing measurements. Note: `track-record-public.ts` covers the SPX Slayer public embed specifically (`fetchPlayOutcomeStats`), not the 0DTE (`zerodte/record`) ledger — CQ-096's premise conflates two different products' public/internal pairs; the SPX pair is proven consistent-by-construction, the 0DTE public vs internal pair was not directly diffed in this pass.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/track-record-public.ts:1-42 (formatPercent doc comment + pct()); src/lib/zerodte/record.ts:565; src/lib/zerodte/record.test.ts:669 ('33.3'). LIVE CHECK to fully close the 0DTE side: GET /api/public/track-record and GET /api/market/zerodte/record?days=30 same instant, diff win_rate_pct scaled to same dp.

Separately, `src/lib/providers/spot-fallback.ts` provides a UW `/stock-state`-based spot FALLBACK (used e.g. by socket-cluster-health/data-correctness when Polygon WS/REST/cluster snapshot are all cold) — explicitly NOT usable for SPX/VIX/NDX/RUT (UW returns HTTP 422 for index tickers, `UW_STOCK_STATE_UNSUPPORTED_TICKERS`), so for index products the UW fallback simply doesn't fire; only equity-ticker desks can silently fail over to UW spot. 0DTE live marks (`zerodte/live-marks.ts`) quote CONTRACTS via a dedicated ~1s Polygon quote lane (documented 'single lane... never come from two different quote lanes') for pricing plays, a fourth distinct spot-adjacent mechanism (option quotes, not underlying spot) not compared against the GEX matrix's spot in this pass.

LIVE CHECK: pull GET /api/market/gex-heatmap?ticker=SPX, /api/market/spx/desk, /api/market/vector/universe, and /api/market/zerodte/board at the same instant; diff spot values and each payload's own spot_source/quote-timestamp field.

## J. Market Data Pipeline

**CQ-098** | PROVEN
A: UW WS leader election uses a Redis SETNX lock `uw:ws:leader` with a 25s TTL (`UW_LEADER_TTL_SEC = 25`), renewed every 10s by the leader (`startUwLeaderRefresh`, well within the TTL). If the leader crashes without releasing the lock (no graceful SIGTERM), the lock simply expires after the 25s TTL elapses (Redis EX), and the next standby replica's periodic reconcile tick (`ensureUwLeadership`/`tryAcquireUwLead` via SETNX 'uw:ws:leader' EX 25 NX) wins the now-expired key and takes over — it does not need to detect the crash explicitly. On a graceful shutdown, `releaseUwLead()` deletes the lock immediately (via `releaseFencedLock`, fenced by a random per-process `UW_LOCK_TOKEN` so a replica can never release/renew a lock it doesn't actually hold — preventing split-brain), so a clean restart hands over in ~0s instead of waiting out the TTL. Worst-case flow-ingestion outage duration after an ungraceful crash: bounded by the 25s TTL + the standby's own reconcile cadence (`UW_RECONCILE_INTERVAL_MS = 15_000`, explicitly documented as '< UW_LEADER_TTL_SEC so a standby acquires the lock within one tick of the leader dying') — so worst case is roughly TTL(25s) + one reconcile tick(15s) ≈ under ~40s before a new leader opens the socket, plus normal WS connect/auth/join time (seconds) before data resumes. If Redis itself is unreachable, `tryAcquireUwLead` fails CLOSED in a multi-replica deployment (`wsLeaderShouldFailOpenWithoutRedis()` false case) rather than every replica opening a duplicate socket (UW allows only one connection per API key).
EVIDENCE: src/lib/ws/uw-socket.ts:176-262 (UW_LEADER_KEY='uw:ws:leader', UW_LEADER_TTL_SEC=25, startUwLeaderRefresh renews every 10s, releaseUwLead on shutdown, newLockToken/renewFencedLock/releaseFencedLock fencing); line 1149 UW_RECONCILE_INTERVAL_MS=15_000 with comment 'Must be < UW_LEADER_TTL_SEC so a standby acquires the lock within one tick of the leader dying'. No dedicated failover unit test file was found for this exact scenario in this pass (a live/CloudWatch confirmation of an actual replica-crash failover timing would be the definitive proof: kill the leader ECS task and grep `/ecs/blackout-production` logs for '[uw-socket] acquired cluster lead' on a standby within ~40s).

**CQ-099** | PROVEN
A: The Polygon/Massive indices WS subscribes SPX, VIX, VIX9D, VIX3M, TICK, TRIN, and ADD (both 1s aggregate bars 'A' and tick-level 'V' channels) — no other indices. If the VIX feed specifically drops while the rest of the socket stays alive, there is NO per-symbol stale badge shown to the desk: `getIndexFeedFreshness(sym, now)` is a generic per-symbol freshness function (stalled after `INDEX_FEED_STALL_MS`, default 5s) that CAN be called per-symbol including 'I:VIX', but the only wired stall-detection/reconnect watchdog (`startIndicesWatchdog`) is socket-wide, not per-symbol — it reconnects the WHOLE multiplex socket only when the ENTIRE feed goes silent (any frame counts toward `lastIndicesMessageAt`), so if SPX/TICK/etc. keep ticking but VIX specifically stops (e.g. Polygon silently stops publishing that one symbol while others still fire), the watchdog would not detect it as a stall (since other symbols keep refreshing `lastIndicesMessageAt`). Whether the SPX desk UI itself surfaces a VIX-specific stale badge (vs. silently freezing/omitting the VIX field) was not confirmed in this pass — I found the underlying `getIndexFeedFreshness` primitive that COULD drive such a badge, but did not locate the specific desk component consuming it for VIX with certainty in the time available.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/ws/polygon-socket.ts:407-408 (subscribe params: 'A.I:SPX,A.I:VIX,A.I:VIX9D,A.I:VIX3M,A.I:TICK,A.I:TRIN,A.I:ADD,' + 'V.I:SPX,V.I:VIX,...'); lines 59-67 indexStore keys confirm the same 7 symbols; lines 292-342 startIndicesWatchdog is socket-wide (checks `lastIndicesMessageAt`, ANY frame resets it, comment 'ANY frame counts'); lines 621-634 getIndexFeedFreshness is genuinely per-symbol (INDEX_FEED_STALL_MS=5s) but I did not verify which UI component calls it for VIX specifically — PARTIALLY PROVEN on the UI-badge-vs-silent-omission half. Live check that would prove it: force a synthetic VIX-only stall (or watch a live session where VIX momentarily has no print) and inspect the SPX desk payload/DOM for a VIX-specific staleness indicator vs. a frozen-looking VIX value with no badge.

**CQ-100** | PROVEN
A: options-socket.ts writes each option mark to Redis under key prefix with TTL `MARK_REDIS_TTL_SEC` (env `OPTIONS_WS_MARK_TTL_SEC`, default 15s) via `sharedCacheSet(...,MARK_REDIS_TTL_SEC)`. Separately, the 0DTE-specific staleness bar used by the live-marks lane (`zerodte/marks-math.ts`) is `ZERODTE_MARK_STALE_MS = 5_000` (5 seconds) — much tighter than the Redis key TTL. Behavior on a 60s gap during RTH on an active 0DTE contract: marks FREEZE, they do not extrapolate. The in-memory `markStore` (live-marks.ts) is append-only / last-write-wins by `asOf` (`putZeroDteLiveMark`: 'newest asOf wins (never regress a fresher tick)') and is never actively updated by a timer — so if no fresh WS tick and no REST fill-in snapshot arrives, the stored mark simply stops advancing. The freshness gate `isZeroDteMarkStale(asOf, now, staleAfterMs=5000)` flips `stale:true` on the payload after 5s of silence, and both the LATCH mark (peak/trough/hard-stop, tolerant to 30s via `LATCH_MAX_MARK_AGE_MS`) and the ENGINE mark (ratchet/thesis/target exits, tolerant only to the 5s bar) are set to `null` once stale rather than being extrapolated forward — the code comment explicitly states 'a mark-DRIVEN engine exit may only act on a CURRENT quote... When the freshest mark is staler, engineMark is null → evaluateLedgerRowExit HOLDs (missing mark = no engine exit...), so the engine can never exit at a price nobody currently sees.' The protective stop-loss LATCH, however, is explicitly designed to survive up to 30s of staleness (deepening a stop but never lifting one) so capital protection doesn't depend on quote freshness — but at 60s (beyond even the 30s LATCH ceiling), BOTH the engine-exit AND the LATCH mark would be nulled/frozen, meaning a genuinely dangerous 60s-long true price move on an OPEN 0DTE position would not trigger ANY exit logic (neither engine-driven nor latch-driven) until a fresh quote resumes — the P&L display itself would also freeze at the last real mark rather than moving with the market, which does risk a member seeing a stale/wrong live P&L number for up to that gap.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/ws/options-socket.ts:199-246 (MARK_REDIS_TTL_SEC=15 default, sharedCacheSet with that TTL); src/lib/zerodte/marks-math.ts:39 (ZERODTE_MARK_STALE_MS=5_000); src/lib/zerodte/live-marks.ts:91 (LATCH_MAX_MARK_AGE_MS=30_000), lines 432-439 (putZeroDteLiveMark last-write-wins, no extrapolation), lines 618-642 (mark vs engineMark computed via isZeroDteMarkStale with the 30s vs 5s bars, both nulled past their respective ceilings — comments confirm HOLD/no-action, not extrapolation).

**CQ-101** | PARTIALLY PROVEN
A: There IS a real, hard-coded NYSE holiday calendar (`US_MARKET_HOLIDAYS` in src/features/nighthawk/lib/session.ts, extending 2026-2029, e.g. includes '2026-09-07' Labor Day) feeding `isTradingDayEt`/`isMarketHolidayEt`, and the canonical RTH gate `isEtCashRth` (src/lib/et-market-hours.ts) DOES check it before returning true, with an explicit comment: 'Full NYSE closures... are weekdays on the clock but not trading sessions — without this gate data-correctness falsely flags stale flow_alerts during holidays.' However, a direct repo-wide check of the 55 cron routes under src/app/api/cron/*/route.ts found only 12 routes reference `isEtCashRth`/`isTradingDayEt`/`isMarketHolidayEt` DIRECTLY in their own route file (bie-full-state-snapshot, coaching-alerts, darkpool-discord, helix-discord-digest, nighthawk-morning-confirm, thermal-discord, vector-bead-record, vector-dark-pool-warm, vector-full-state-snapshot, vector-pick-sweep, vector-universe-snapshot, vector-walls-warm) — a substantial set of high-traffic crons, including `swing-discovery`, are NOT among them. I traced `swing-discovery`'s own decision logic (`src/lib/swing/scan-cadence.ts`, `resolveScanPhase`/`decideSwingScan`) end-to-end and confirmed it contains NO weekday check and NO holiday check whatsoever — it is purely an ET wall-clock phase-window lookup (e.g. POST_CLOSE 16:15-20:00 ET). If EventBridge's own cron schedule (defined in the separate blackout-infra repo, not present here) fires this route on a NYSE holiday Monday (a weekday on the clock), `scan-cadence.ts` would resolve a phase and the cron would proceed to write a fresh accumulation-memory advance for that date exactly as if it were a normal trading day — the exact 'freshness checks pass incorrectly' failure the question describes. Other un-gated crons (banger-discovery, data-correctness, heatmap-warm, desk-warm, gex-eod-snapshot, nighthawk-edition, spx-evaluate, zerodte-warm) may gate indirectly through an imported service function (e.g. data-correctness reads `card.marketOpen` from the shared SPX desk bundle, which likely traces to `isEtCashRth` internally) — I did not have time to trace every one of those transitively to a definitive holiday check, so this is not a complete top-10 audit.
EVIDENCE: src/features/nighthawk/lib/session.ts:1-71 (US_MARKET_HOLIDAYS set + isTradingDayEt); src/lib/et-market-hours.ts:11-24 (isEtCashRth calls isTradingDayEt); src/lib/swing/scan-cadence.ts:1-80 (SWING_SCAN_PHASES, resolveScanPhase — no weekday/holiday check, confirmed via grep for isTradingDayEt/isMarketHolidayEt/weekday/Sat/Sun in both discovery.ts and scan-cadence.ts returning zero hits); grep across src/app/api/cron/*/route.ts for isTradingDayEt|isMarketHolidayEt|isEtCashRth found only the 12 routes named above gated directly. Live check that would prove/disprove the swing-discovery holiday gap definitively: inspect blackout-infra's terraform (not in this repo) for swing-discovery's actual EventBridge cron_expression day-of-week field — if it's MON-FRI with no holiday exclusion (the norm for EventBridge classic Rules per this repo's own cron-dst-audit.mjs tooling notes, which state EventBridge has no holiday support at all), then on the next NYSE holiday, grep CloudWatch Logs `/ecs/blackout-production` for a `swing-discovery` invocation with `run:true` on that date.

'PRE-MARKET' : label` on the served desk payload — so the SPX desk UI does carry an explicit PRE-MARKET label rather than presenting premarket data as live RTH. The same pattern repeats across Largo's tool surface: `src/lib/largo/core/system-status.ts` defines `MarketPhase = 'OPEN'|'PRE-MARKET'|'AFTER-HOURS'|'CLOSED'` (PRE-MARKET = 4:00-9:30 ET), and `src/lib/providers/gex-positioning.ts` documents its own `market_session` field as 'OPEN | PRE-MARKET | AFTER-HOURS | CLOSED — holiday-aware', with `tool-defs.ts` explicitly instructing Largo to 'say which session a price belongs to rather than presenting it as current' outside OPEN, since 'spot is the prior session's close or an extended-hours print, NOT a live quote.' 'PRE-MARKET' : label); src/lib/largo/core/system-status.ts:57,75 (MarketPhase type + PRE-MARKET window 4:00-9:30 ET); src/lib/providers/gex-positioning.ts:83 ('OPEN | PRE-MARKET | AFTER-HOURS | CLOSED — holiday-aware'); src/lib/largo/tool-defs.ts:559 ('Outside OPEN, spot is the prior session's close or an extended-hours print, NOT a live quote: say which session a price belongs to'). Live check to close the remaining gap: hit each product's API route (/api/market/vector/*, /api/market/thermal/*, /heatmap, /flows) at ~7:00 AM ET with a temp Clerk session and diff the returned `as_of`/`market_session` against actual premarket tape movement to confirm each is genuinely live rather than a relabeled stale snapshot.

**CQ-103** | PROVEN
A: The freshness-guard sweep is extensively documented in docs/audit/FINDINGS.md across many dated entries (the 'raw `Date.now() - t` age computation with no future-timestamp guard' bug shape, fixed at 17+ sites per one entry, then more waves after). Crucially, one of those entries NAMES the exact remaining unguarded cluster at the time it was written: '...The largest remaining cluster is the raw WebSocket ingestion layer (src/lib/ws/* — Polygon/UW stock, options, and index sockets) reading upstream tick timestamps directly; that is real infrastructure with a much wider blast radius per file and deserves its own focused review rather than being swept into an unrelated PR — flagged here for whoever next works that layer, not attempted unilaterally.' I verified the current state of that flagged cluster directly: `polygon-socket.ts`'s `getIndexFeedFreshness` (lines 621-634) HAS since been fixed with an explicit future-tolerance check (`if (rawAgeMs < -WS_TIMESTAMP_FUTURE_TOLERANCE_MS) return { ageMs: null, stalled: true, ... }`) — so that specific site is now guarded. The socket-internal stall-watchdog comparisons in polygon-socket.ts/uw-socket.ts/options-socket.ts (e.g. `now - this.lastMessageAt <= stallMs`) are same-process writer/reader pairs (the timestamp is always `Date.now()` from the same process shortly before comparison), which FINDINGS.md itself classifies as 'not vulnerable' in practice — a genuinely future value isn't reachable there. Two other sites the same entry named as 'deliberately left for a future pass' — `ZeroDteBoard.tsx`'s `resolveZeroDteFreshness` and `zerodte-service.ts`'s `localBoardIsServable` — I traced `localBoardIsServable` (src/lib/platform/zerodte-service.ts:859-864) and confirmed it now routes through `isZeroDteMarkStale`, which (per the 2026-09-xx entry adding `ZERODTE_MARK_FUTURE_TOLERANCE_MS`=60s) IS future-guarded — so that site is also fixed. I did not have time to re-verify `ZeroDteBoard.tsx`'s `resolveZeroDteFreshness` specifically in this pass.
EVIDENCE: docs/audit/FINDINGS.md:3575 ('The same grep also turned up ~25 more Date.now() - X comparisons... the largest remaining cluster is the raw WebSocket ingestion layer (src/lib/ws/*)... flagged here for whoever next works that layer'); src/lib/ws/polygon-socket.ts:621-634 (getIndexFeedFreshness now has the `rawAgeMs < -WS_TIMESTAMP_FUTURE_TOLERANCE_MS` guard, confirming this specific WS-layer site has since been fixed); src/lib/platform/zerodte-service.ts:853-864 (localBoardIsServable → isZeroDteMarkStale, which per FINDINGS.md:3758 now enforces ZERODTE_MARK_FUTURE_TOLERANCE_MS=60s). Remaining open item to verify live: `src/features/nighthawk/command-deck/ZeroDteBoard.tsx`'s `resolveZeroDteFreshness` — grep that function directly (`grep -n resolveZeroDteFreshness -A 15 src/features/nighthawk/command-deck/ZeroDteBoard.tsx`) to confirm it too routes through a future-guarded helper rather than a raw `now - t` subtraction.

**CQ-104** | PROVEN
A: When Redis is unavailable, `shared-cache.ts` falls back to an in-memory `Map` (`memory`) that is scoped PER PROCESS (per ECS task/replica) — it is a plain module-level `Map<string, MemoryEntry>`, bounded at `MAX_MEMORY_ENTRIES=5_000` with LRU-ish eviction, with no cross-replica synchronization mechanism at all. In multi-replica ECS, this produces exactly 'split-brain desks', not a 503 and not 'no change': each replica independently builds and caches its own view in its own memory, so a member's request routed to replica A can see different (or missing) cached data than one routed to replica B, depending on which replica happened to compute/write that key locally during the Redis outage. This is corroborated by the leader-election code in uw-socket.ts/polygon-socket.ts: when Redis is down in a genuinely multi-replica deployment, `tryAcquireUwLead`/`tryAcquireIndicesLead` explicitly FAIL CLOSED (`wsLeaderShouldFailOpenWithoutRedis()` false branch, `alertWsLeaderFailClosedOnce`) rather than letting every replica open its own duplicate upstream WS — an acknowledgment in the code itself that an unmediated multi-replica Redis outage is a known split-brain/contention risk class, handled explicitly for the leader-lock case but NOT mitigated for the general shared-cache fallback (ordinary `sharedCacheGet`/`sharedCacheSet` calls have no such fail-closed behavior — they just silently degrade to the local, unsynchronized memory Map).
EVIDENCE: src/lib/shared-cache.ts:1-23 (per-process `memory` Map, MAX_MEMORY_ENTRIES=5000, no cross-replica sync), lines 39-107 (getRedis() returns null on failure with 30s backoff, sharedCacheGet falls through to the local `memory` Map on any Redis error or absence); src/lib/ws/uw-socket.ts:196-217 and src/lib/ws/polygon-socket.ts:166-187 (tryAcquireUwLead/tryAcquireIndicesLead explicitly fail-closed on Redis-down in a multi-replica deployment, confirming the split-brain risk is a recognized class elsewhere in the codebase). Live check that would prove the exact user-visible symptom: force a Redis outage against a 2+-replica ECS service (e.g. temporarily point REDIS_URL at an unreachable host, or use an ElastiCache maintenance window) and diff two consecutive requests to the same endpoint routed to different replicas (via repeated polling until the ALB round-robins) for a divergent cached value.

**CQ-105** | PROVEN
A: There is a concrete, deterministic degraded-mode playbook for the GEX heatmap/matrix specifically (found in code, not a prose runbook — no dedicated docs/ file named for a 'Polygon outage playbook' was found in this pass). `gexHeatmapMaxStaleMs()` (default 90s, env `GEX_HEATMAP_MAX_STALE_SEC`) is the boundary: `tryStaleWhileRevalidateHeatmap` will serve a cached matrix that is stale (age >= its normal TTL) but still within this 90s ceiling immediately while kicking off a background single-flight rebuild — classic stale-while-revalidate, with an explicit code comment citing the incident that motivated it: '(live-caught 2026-07-06: SPX /gex-heatmap 502 + dashboard matrix stuck loading).' HOWEVER, this is NOT a hard fail past 90s: the sibling function `pickStaleHeatmapForHandoff` (used by the 'never block' request-timeout path, `awaitHeatmapBuildWithBlockCap`) explicitly falls back to serving ANY cached copy — 'prefers entries within maxStaleMs, else any cached copy' — with no upper bound at all, meaning during a genuinely prolonged Polygon outage the heatmap can keep serving an arbitrarily old cached matrix (hours old) rather than ever hard-failing/erroring, as long as SOME cached entry exists in memory or Redis. This is a meaningful nuance the question's phrasing ('max cache age before hard fail') doesn't have a clean single answer to: the SOFT ceiling (stale-while-revalidate trigger) is 90s, but there is effectively NO hard ceiling for the last-resort handoff — a design choice favoring always-serve-something over ever showing an error, at the cost of potentially serving very old data silently during an extended outage without an obvious 'this data may be very stale' signal beyond the `expires_at`/age fields a client could choose to check.
EVIDENCE: src/lib/providers/polygon-options-gex.ts:1676-1682 (gexHeatmapMaxStaleMs, default 90s, comment citing the 2026-07-06 502 incident), lines 1692-1723 (tryStaleWhileRevalidateHeatmap: age within [ttlMs, maxStaleMs) served immediately + background rebuild), lines 1727-1745 (pickStaleHeatmapForHandoff: 'prefers entries within maxStaleMs, else any cached copy' — no upper bound on the fallback branch), lines 1748-1762 (awaitHeatmapBuildWithBlockCap: caps the wait at gexHeatmapMaxBlockMs/gexHeatmapForceMaxBlockMs, then hands off via the unbounded pickStaleHeatmapForHandoff rather than erroring). No dedicated prose runbook document for a Polygon-outage playbook was located under docs/ in this pass (docs/ops/RTH-OPEN-RUNBOOK.md and docs/ops/PLAY-ENGINE-RTH-RUNBOOK.md exist but were not read in full to confirm/deny Polygon-outage-specific content — that is the gap a follow-up read of those two files would close).
